**ChatGPT:**

Yes. I found exactly what happened, and this is actually good news.

The old runner is stale because it already succeeded in committing and pushing the Stage 4.1 patch. Your latest failure checks whether HEAD is still the pre-commit reviewed base fbe193f..., but your HEAD is now b6f21af..., so it stops with Reviewed base HEAD changed. 

I verified this directly against GitHub:

b6f21afdf87e3c4309f59f832dd19859a3bc7d7c is exactly Harden TaskReviewAgent repository binding.
Its parent is exactly the reviewed base fbe193f9578f02110005c78a72f7ef0d6a7fff06.
It contains exactly the eight reviewed Stage 4.1 files.
The remote stage4-repository-binding-safety branch already points to b6f21af....
main is still exactly fbe193f..., so nobody advanced the integration base.
There is currently no PR at all for the Stage 4.1 branch.

So do not commit again. We are here:

reviewed main
fbe193f...
    │
    ▼
Stage 4.1 commit
b6f21af...    ← local HEAD
    │
    └──────────── remote branch already pushed

PR:   NOT CREATED YET
CI:   NOT RUN YET
main: still fbe193f...

The build history confirms that the intended flow was commit → push → PR → exact-SHA CI → merge.  We simply got partway through it.

Use this replacement runner

This one deliberately starts from the already-created exact commit. It will not stage or create another commit. It verifies the exact patch, creates/reuses the PR, waits for exact-SHA CI, merges only if main is still the reviewed base, and updates local main.

It is also safe to rerun if it stops later: it can reuse an open PR or recognize an already-merged PR.

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE = "1"
    $env:PYTHONUNBUFFERED = "1"

    # ============================================================
    # STAGE 4.1 — RESUME-SAFE DELIVERY
    #
    # CURRENT REAL STATE:
    #   reviewed base committed already
    #   Stage 4.1 commit created already
    #   branch pushed already
    #
    # THIS RUNNER:
    #   VERIFY COMMIT -> PR -> EXACT-SHA CI -> MERGE -> UPDATE MAIN
    # ============================================================

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\NoSafeCircle"
    $Branch = "stage4-repository-binding-safety"

    $ExpectedRepository = "cathode26/NoSafeCircle"

    $ExpectedBaseHead =
        "fbe193f9578f02110005c78a72f7ef0d6a7fff06"

    $ExpectedPatchCommit =
        "b6f21afdf87e3c4309f59f832dd19859a3bc7d7c"

    $ExpectedCommitMessage =
        "Harden TaskReviewAgent repository binding"

    $PrTitle =
        "Harden TaskReviewAgent repository binding"

    $RequiredWorkflow =
        "TaskReviewAgent Deterministic Validation"

    $RequiredJob =
        "windows-smoke"

    $ExpectedFiles = @(
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/downstream_pipeline.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/downstream_runtime.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/durable_checkout.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/issue_workflow_store.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/real_checkout.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/downstream_smoke_test.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/issue_workflow_smoke_test.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/real_checkout_smoke_test.py"
    ) | Sort-Object

    $Stamp = Get-Date -Format "yyyyMMdd-HHmmss"

    $PrBodyPath = Join-Path `
        $env:TEMP `
        "nsc-stage4-repository-binding-pr-$Stamp.md"

    # ============================================================
    # WINDOWS POWERSHELL 5.1 SAFE NATIVE HELPERS
    # ============================================================

    function Invoke-GitSafe {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments,

            [int[]]$AllowedExitCodes = @(0),

            [switch]$Capture
        )

        $OldPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

        try {
            $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

            if ($Capture) {
                $Output = @(& git @Arguments)
            }
            else {
                & git @Arguments | Out-Host
                $Output = @()
            }

            $Code = $LASTEXITCODE
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $OldPreference
        }

        if ($AllowedExitCodes -notcontains $Code) {
            throw (
                "git " +
                ($Arguments -join " ") +
                " failed with exit code $Code."
            )
        }

        return [pscustomobject]@{
            ExitCode = $Code
            Output   = $Output
        }
    }

    function Invoke-GhSafe {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments,

            [int[]]$AllowedExitCodes = @(0),

            [switch]$Capture
        )

        $OldPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

        try {
            $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

            if ($Capture) {
                $Output = @(& gh @Arguments)
            }
            else {
                & gh @Arguments | Out-Host
                $Output = @()
            }

            $Code = $LASTEXITCODE
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $OldPreference
        }

        if ($AllowedExitCodes -notcontains $Code) {
            throw (
                "gh " +
                ($Arguments -join " ") +
                " failed with exit code $Code."
            )
        }

        return [pscustomobject]@{
            ExitCode = $Code
            Output   = $Output
        }
    }

    function Get-GitText {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments
        )

        $Result = Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments $Arguments `
            -Capture

        return (
            ($Result.Output -join "`n").Trim()
        )
    }

    function Get-GhText {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments
        )

        $Result = Invoke-GhSafe `
            -Arguments $Arguments `
            -Capture

        return (
            ($Result.Output -join "`n").Trim()
        )
    }

    function Get-NormalizedPaths {
        param(
            [object[]]$Lines
        )

        return @(
            $Lines |
            ForEach-Object {
                ([string]$_).Replace("\", "/")
            } |
            Where-Object {
                -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
            } |
            Sort-Object
        )
    }

    Set-Location $Root

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "[START] RESUME STAGE 4.1 DELIVERY" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "[STATE] Reviewed base: $ExpectedBaseHead"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Patch commit:  $ExpectedPatchCommit"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Branch:        $Branch"
    Write-Host ""

    # ============================================================
    # 1. VERIFY WE ARE ON THE EXACT ALREADY-COMMITTED PATCH
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host "[VERIFY] Local branch and exact patch commit" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $CurrentBranch = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "branch",
            "--show-current"
        )

    if ($CurrentBranch -ne $Branch) {
        throw (
            "Expected branch '$Branch'. " +
            "Current branch is '$CurrentBranch'."
        )
    }

    $CurrentHead = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "rev-parse",
            "HEAD"
        )

    if ($CurrentHead -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
        throw @"
Unexpected local HEAD.

Expected the already-created Stage 4.1 patch commit:

$ExpectedPatchCommit

Current HEAD:

$CurrentHead

Do not create another commit.
"@
    }

    $Status = @(
        (Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            ) `
            -Capture).Output
    )

    if ($Status.Count -ne 0) {
        Write-Host "[BLOCKED] Working tree is not clean:" -ForegroundColor Red
        $Status | ForEach-Object {
            Write-Host "  $_"
        }

        throw (
            "The already-created Stage 4.1 commit must have a clean tree."
        )
    }

    Write-Host "[PASS] Local HEAD is exact Stage 4.1 commit" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[PASS] Working tree is clean" -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 2. PROVE COMMIT IDENTITY
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[VERIFY] Commit parent, message, and exact file scope" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $Parent = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "rev-parse",
            "$ExpectedPatchCommit^"
        )

    if ($Parent -ne $ExpectedBaseHead) {
        throw @"
Stage 4.1 commit has the wrong parent.

Expected:
$ExpectedBaseHead

Actual:
$Parent
"@
    }

    $CommitSubject = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "show",
            "-s",
            "--format=%s",
            $ExpectedPatchCommit
        )

    if ($CommitSubject -ne $ExpectedCommitMessage) {
        throw @"
Stage 4.1 commit message differs from the reviewed delivery commit.

Expected:
$ExpectedCommitMessage

Actual:
$CommitSubject
"@
    }

    $CommitFilesRaw = @(
        (Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "diff-tree",
                "--no-commit-id",
                "--name-only",
                "-r",
                $ExpectedPatchCommit
            ) `
            -Capture).Output
    )

    $CommitFiles = Get-NormalizedPaths `
        -Lines $CommitFilesRaw

    $CommitScopeDiff = @(
        Compare-Object `
            $ExpectedFiles `
            $CommitFiles
    )

    if ($CommitScopeDiff.Count -ne 0) {
        Write-Host "[BLOCKED] Stage 4.1 commit scope differs from review." -ForegroundColor Red

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[EXPECTED]"
        $ExpectedFiles |
            ForEach-Object {
                Write-Host "  $_"
            }

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[ACTUAL]"
        $CommitFiles |
            ForEach-Object {
                Write-Host "  $_"
            }

        throw "Refusing delivery of a different commit."
    }

    if ($CommitFiles -contains "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/claim_refs.py") {
        throw "claim_refs.py unexpectedly appears in the Stage 4.1 commit."
    }

    $CommitWhitespaceArgs = @(
        "-c",
        "core.whitespace=cr-at-eol",
        "-c",
        "core.safecrlf=false",
        "diff",
        "--check",
        "$ExpectedBaseHead..$ExpectedPatchCommit",
        "--"
    ) + $ExpectedFiles

    Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments $CommitWhitespaceArgs |
        Out-Null

    Write-Host "[PASS] Commit parent is exact reviewed base" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[PASS] Commit message is exact" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[PASS] Commit contains exactly eight reviewed files" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[PASS] claim_refs.py is absent from commit" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[PASS] Committed whitespace check" -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 3. VERIFY GITHUB IDENTITY
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[VERIFY] GitHub authentication and repository identity" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    Invoke-GhSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "auth",
            "status"
        ) |
        Out-Null

    $Repository = Get-GhText `
        -Arguments @(
            "repo",
            "view",
            "--json",
            "nameWithOwner",
            "--jq",
            ".nameWithOwner"
        )

    if ($Repository -ne $ExpectedRepository) {
        throw (
            "Current checkout resolves to GitHub repository " +
            "'$Repository', expected '$ExpectedRepository'."
        )
    }

    Write-Host "[PASS] GitHub repository: $Repository" -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 4. VERIFY REMOTE BRANCH CONTAINS EXACT PATCH
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[VERIFY] Remote Stage 4.1 branch" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $RemoteBranchLines = @(
        (Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "ls-remote",
                "--heads",
                "origin",
                "refs/heads/$Branch"
            ) `
            -Capture).Output |
        Where-Object {
            -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace([string]$_)
        }
    )

    if ($RemoteBranchLines.Count -eq 0) {
        Write-Host "[STATE] Remote branch is missing; pushing exact existing commit." -ForegroundColor Yellow

        Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "push",
                "-u",
                "origin",
                $Branch
            ) |
            Out-Null

        $RemoteBranchLines = @(
            (Invoke-GitSafe `
                -Arguments @(
                    "ls-remote",
                    "--heads",
                    "origin",
                    "refs/heads/$Branch"
                ) `
                -Capture).Output |
            Where-Object {
                -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace([string]$_)
            }
        )
    }

    if ($RemoteBranchLines.Count -ne 1) {
        throw "Could not establish exactly one remote Stage 4.1 branch."
    }

    $RemoteBranchSha = (
        ([string]$RemoteBranchLines[0] -split "\s+")[0]
    ).Trim()

    if ($RemoteBranchSha -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
        throw @"
Remote branch is not the reviewed Stage 4.1 commit.

Expected:
$ExpectedPatchCommit

Remote:
$RemoteBranchSha
"@
    }

    Write-Host "[PASS] origin/$Branch == $ExpectedPatchCommit" -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 5. LOCATE ANY EXISTING PR
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[READ] Looking for existing Stage 4.1 PR" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $ExistingPrJson = Get-GhText `
        -Arguments @(
            "pr",
            "list",
            "--repo",
            $Repository,
            "--head",
            $Branch,
            "--state",
            "all",
            "--limit",
            "10",
            "--json",
            "number,url,state,headRefOid"
        )

    if (
        [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($ExistingPrJson) -or
        $ExistingPrJson.Trim() -eq "[]"
    ) {
        $ExistingPrs = @()
    }
    else {
        $ExistingPrs = @(
            $ExistingPrJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json
        )
    }

    if ($ExistingPrs.Count -gt 1) {
        throw (
            "More than one pull request exists for branch '$Branch'."
        )
    }

    $PrNumber = $null
    $PrUrl = $null
    $PrAlreadyMerged = $false
    $MergeSha = $null

    if ($ExistingPrs.Count -eq 1) {
        $PrNumber = [int]$ExistingPrs[0].number
        $PrUrl = [string]$ExistingPrs[0].url
        $ExistingState = [string]$ExistingPrs[0].state
        $ExistingHead = [string]$ExistingPrs[0].headRefOid

        if ($ExistingHead -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "Existing PR #$PrNumber points to a different head SHA."
            )
        }

        if ($ExistingState -eq "CLOSED") {
            throw (
                "Existing PR #$PrNumber was closed without merge. " +
                "Refusing to silently create a replacement."
            )
        }

        if ($ExistingState -eq "MERGED") {
            $PrAlreadyMerged = $true
            Write-Host "[STATE] PR #$PrNumber is already merged." -ForegroundColor Yellow
        }
        elseif ($ExistingState -eq "OPEN") {
            Write-Host "[STATE] Reusing open PR #$PrNumber" -ForegroundColor Green
        }
        else {
            throw (
                "Unexpected existing PR state '$ExistingState'."
            )
        }
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 6. IF NOT ALREADY MERGED, MAIN MUST STILL BE REVIEWED BASE
    # ============================================================

    if (-not $PrAlreadyMerged) {

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[READ] Fetching current origin/main" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "main"
            ) |
            Out-Null

        $OriginMain = Get-GitText `
            -Arguments @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/main"
            )

        if ($OriginMain -ne $ExpectedBaseHead) {
            Write-Host "[BLOCKED] main advanced since Stage 4.1 review." -ForegroundColor Red
            Write-Host "[REVIEWED BASE] $ExpectedBaseHead"
            Write-Host "[CURRENT MAIN]  $OriginMain"

            throw (
                "Do not merge Stage 4.1 without integrating and " +
                "revalidating the newer main."
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] origin/main still equals reviewed base" -ForegroundColor Green
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 7. CREATE PR IF NONE EXISTS
    # ============================================================

    if ($null -eq $PrNumber) {

        $PrBody = @"
## Stage 4.1 — Repository Binding Safety

This patch removes implicit production-repository authority from active
TaskReviewAgent Issue, checkout, and downstream PR/Issue paths.

### Guarantees

- source checkout origin binds GhIssueBackend repository
- explicit repository values are assertions, not fallback authority
- real/durable/resumable task checkouts are bound to controller origin
- cross-repository task checkouts fail closed
- downstream PR/Issue commands use the checkout-bound repository
- checkout/backend mismatch fails before gh invocation
- credential-bearing rejected origins are redacted
- missing controller origin fails closed
- Stage 1 claim semantics remain unchanged
- claim_refs.py has zero functional change
- Stage 5 remains outside scope
- autonomous dispatch remains disabled

### Review

Final adversarial Fable verdict:

STAGE 4 REPOSITORY BINDING READY TO COMMIT

Reviewed base:

$ExpectedBaseHead

Exact Stage 4.1 commit:

$ExpectedPatchCommit
"@

        [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText(
            $PrBodyPath,
            (($PrBody -replace "`r`n", "`n") -replace "`r", "`n"),
            (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false))
        )

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WORK] Creating Stage 4.1 pull request" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-GhSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "create",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--base",
                "main",
                "--head",
                $Branch,
                "--title",
                $PrTitle,
                "--body-file",
                $PrBodyPath
            ) |
            Out-Null

        $CreatedPrJson = Get-GhText `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "list",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--head",
                $Branch,
                "--state",
                "open",
                "--json",
                "number,url,state,headRefOid"
            )

        $CreatedPrs = @(
            $CreatedPrJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json
        )

        if ($CreatedPrs.Count -ne 1) {
            throw (
                "Could not resolve exactly one newly created Stage 4.1 PR."
            )
        }

        $PrNumber = [int]$CreatedPrs[0].number
        $PrUrl = [string]$CreatedPrs[0].url

        if ([string]$CreatedPrs[0].headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "New PR does not point to exact Stage 4.1 commit."
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Created PR #$($PrNumber): $PrUrl" -ForegroundColor Green
    }
    elseif (-not $PrAlreadyMerged) {
        Write-Host "[PASS] PR #$($PrNumber): $PrUrl" -ForegroundColor Green
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 8. EXACT-SHA CI
    # ============================================================

    if (-not $PrAlreadyMerged) {

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WAIT] Waiting for exact Stage 4.1 CI check" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $FoundRequiredCheck = $false

        for ($Attempt = 1; $Attempt -le 90; $Attempt++) {

            $PrStateJson = Get-GhText `
                -Arguments @(
                    "pr",
                    "view",
                    "$PrNumber",
                    "--repo",
                    $Repository,
                    "--json",
                    "headRefOid,statusCheckRollup,state,url"
                )

            $PrState = $PrStateJson |
                ConvertFrom-Json

            if ([string]$PrState.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
                throw (
                    "PR head changed while waiting for CI."
                )
            }

            if ([string]$PrState.state -ne "OPEN") {
                throw (
                    "PR stopped being open while waiting for CI."
                )
            }

            $Checks = @(
                $PrState.statusCheckRollup
            )

            $Required = @(
                $Checks |
                Where-Object {
                    $null -ne $_ -and
                    $_.__typename -eq "CheckRun" -and
                    $_.name -eq $RequiredJob -and
                    $_.workflowName -eq $RequiredWorkflow
                }
            )

            if ($Required.Count -gt 0) {
                $FoundRequiredCheck = $true

                Write-Host (
                    "[PASS] Required exact-SHA CI appeared on " +
                    $ExpectedPatchCommit
                ) -ForegroundColor Green

                break
            }

            if (($Attempt % 6) -eq 0) {
                Write-Host (
                    "[WAIT] Required CI not visible yet " +
                    "($Attempt/90)"
                ) -ForegroundColor Yellow
            }

            Start-Sleep -Seconds 5
        }

        if (-not $FoundRequiredCheck) {
            throw (
                "Required TaskReviewAgent deterministic CI " +
                "never appeared on the exact patch SHA."
            )
        }

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WAIT] Watching all PR checks" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-GhSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "checks",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--watch",
                "--fail-fast",
                "--interval",
                "10"
            ) |
            Out-Null

        # ========================================================
        # 9. VERIFY EXACT CHECK CONCLUSIONS
        # ========================================================

        $FinalPrJson = Get-GhText `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "headRefOid,statusCheckRollup,state,mergeable,mergeStateStatus,url"
            )

        $FinalPr = $FinalPrJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json

        if ([string]$FinalPr.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "PR head changed after CI."
            )
        }

        $FinalChecks = @(
            $FinalPr.statusCheckRollup
        )

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[STATE] Exact-head checks:" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        foreach ($Check in $FinalChecks) {

            if ($null -eq $Check) {
                continue
            }

            if ($Check.__typename -eq "CheckRun") {
                Write-Host (
                    "  " +
                    $Check.workflowName +
                    "/" +
                    $Check.name +
                    " status=" +
                    $Check.status +
                    " conclusion=" +
                    $Check.conclusion
                )
            }
            else {
                Write-Host (
                    "  " +
                    $Check.context +
                    " state=" +
                    $Check.state
                )
            }
        }

        $RequiredFinal = @(
            $FinalChecks |
            Where-Object {
                $null -ne $_ -and
                $_.__typename -eq "CheckRun" -and
                $_.name -eq $RequiredJob -and
                $_.workflowName -eq $RequiredWorkflow
            }
        )

        if ($RequiredFinal.Count -lt 1) {
            throw (
                "Required deterministic validation check disappeared."
            )
        }

        $RequiredFailures = @(
            $RequiredFinal |
            Where-Object {
                [string]$_.conclusion -ne "SUCCESS"
            }
        )

        if ($RequiredFailures.Count -gt 0) {
            throw (
                "Required deterministic validation did not conclude SUCCESS."
            )
        }

        $BadCheckConclusions = @(
            "FAILURE",
            "CANCELLED",
            "TIMED_OUT",
            "ACTION_REQUIRED",
            "STALE",
            "STARTUP_FAILURE"
        )

        $BadCheckRuns = @(
            $FinalChecks |
            Where-Object {
                $null -ne $_ -and
                $_.__typename -eq "CheckRun" -and
                $_.conclusion -in $BadCheckConclusions
            }
        )

        if ($BadCheckRuns.Count -gt 0) {
            throw (
                "At least one exact-head GitHub check has a failing conclusion."
            )
        }

        $BadStatuses = @(
            $FinalChecks |
            Where-Object {
                $null -ne $_ -and
                $_.__typename -eq "StatusContext" -and
                $_.state -in @(
                    "ERROR",
                    "FAILURE"
                )
            }
        )

        if ($BadStatuses.Count -gt 0) {
            throw (
                "At least one exact-head status context failed."
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Exact-SHA CI is green" -ForegroundColor Green

        # ========================================================
        # 10. MAIN MUST STILL BE THE REVIEWED BASE
        # ========================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[READ] Rechecking main immediately before merge" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "main"
            ) |
            Out-Null

        $OriginMainBeforeMerge = Get-GitText `
            -Arguments @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/main"
            )

        if ($OriginMainBeforeMerge -ne $ExpectedBaseHead) {
            Write-Host "[BLOCKED] main advanced while CI was running." -ForegroundColor Red
            Write-Host "[REVIEWED BASE] $ExpectedBaseHead"
            Write-Host "[CURRENT MAIN]  $OriginMainBeforeMerge"
            Write-Host "[PR] $PrUrl"

            throw (
                "Do not merge without integrating and " +
                "revalidating the newer main."
            )
        }

        # ========================================================
        # 11. FINAL PR HEAD / MERGEABILITY CHECK
        # ========================================================

        $PreMergeJson = Get-GhText `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "headRefOid,state,mergeable,mergeStateStatus"
            )

        $PreMerge = $PreMergeJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json

        if ([string]$PreMerge.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "PR head changed immediately before merge."
            )
        }

        if ([string]$PreMerge.state -ne "OPEN") {
            throw (
                "PR is no longer open immediately before merge."
            )
        }

        if ([string]$PreMerge.mergeable -eq "CONFLICTING") {
            throw (
                "PR is conflicting immediately before merge."
            )
        }

        Write-Host (
            "[PASS] PR head and main still match reviewed integration state"
        ) -ForegroundColor Green

        # ========================================================
        # 12. MERGE EXACT HEAD
        # ========================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WORK] Merging PR #$PrNumber" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-GhSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "merge",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--merge",
                "--match-head-commit",
                $ExpectedPatchCommit
            ) |
            Out-Null

        # ========================================================
        # 13. PROVE MERGED
        # ========================================================

        $MergedJson = Get-GhText `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "state,mergedAt,mergeCommit,headRefOid,url"
            )

        $MergedPr = $MergedJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json

        if ([string]$MergedPr.state -ne "MERGED") {
            throw (
                "PR did not reach MERGED state."
            )
        }

        if ([string]$MergedPr.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "Merged PR head differs from exact reviewed patch commit."
            )
        }

        $MergeSha = [string]$MergedPr.mergeCommit.oid

        if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($MergeSha)) {
            throw (
                "GitHub did not report a merge commit SHA."
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] PR merged" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "[STATE] Merge commit: $MergeSha"
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 14. IF PR WAS ALREADY MERGED, RESOLVE ITS MERGE SHA
    # ============================================================

    if ($PrAlreadyMerged) {

        $MergedJson = Get-GhText `
            -Arguments @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "state,mergedAt,mergeCommit,headRefOid,url"
            )

        $MergedPr = $MergedJson |
            ConvertFrom-Json

        if ([string]$MergedPr.state -ne "MERGED") {
            throw (
                "PR was expected to be merged but GitHub disagrees."
            )
        }

        if ([string]$MergedPr.headRefOid -ne $ExpectedPatchCommit) {
            throw (
                "Merged PR does not contain exact Stage 4.1 head."
            )
        }

        $MergeSha = [string]$MergedPr.mergeCommit.oid

        if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($MergeSha)) {
            throw (
                "Could not resolve existing merge commit."
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Existing merged PR verified" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "[STATE] Merge commit: $MergeSha"
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 15. VERIFY REMOTE MAIN CONTAINS PATCH + MERGE
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[VERIFY] Remote main contains Stage 4.1" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "fetch",
            "origin",
            "main"
        ) |
        Out-Null

    $PatchContained = Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "merge-base",
            "--is-ancestor",
            $ExpectedPatchCommit,
            "origin/main"
        ) `
        -AllowedExitCodes @(
            0,
            1
        )

    if ($PatchContained.ExitCode -ne 0) {
        throw (
            "Exact Stage 4.1 patch commit is not contained in origin/main."
        )
    }

    $MergeContained = Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "merge-base",
            "--is-ancestor",
            $MergeSha,
            "origin/main"
        ) `
        -AllowedExitCodes @(
            0,
            1
        )

    if ($MergeContained.ExitCode -ne 0) {
        throw (
            "Reported Stage 4.1 merge commit is not contained in origin/main."
        )
    }

    Write-Host (
        "[PASS] Exact Stage 4.1 patch commit is contained in origin/main"
    ) -ForegroundColor Green

    Write-Host (
        "[PASS] Stage 4.1 merge commit is contained in origin/main"
    ) -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 16. UPDATE LOCAL MAIN
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[WORK] Updating local main" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "switch",
            "main"
        ) |
        Out-Null

    Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "pull",
            "--ff-only",
            "origin",
            "main"
        ) |
        Out-Null

    $LocalMain = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "rev-parse",
            "HEAD"
        )

    $OriginMainFinal = Get-GitText `
        -Arguments @(
            "rev-parse",
            "origin/main"
        )

    if ($LocalMain -ne $OriginMainFinal) {
        throw (
            "Local main does not exactly match origin/main."
        )
    }

    $LocalPatchContained = Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "merge-base",
            "--is-ancestor",
            $ExpectedPatchCommit,
            "HEAD"
        ) `
        -AllowedExitCodes @(
            0,
            1
        )

    if ($LocalPatchContained.ExitCode -ne 0) {
        throw (
            "Exact Stage 4.1 patch commit is not contained in local main."
        )
    }

    $FinalStatus = @(
        (Invoke-GitSafe `
            -Arguments @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            ) `
            -Capture).Output
    )

    if ($FinalStatus.Count -ne 0) {
        Write-Host "[BLOCKED] Local main is not clean:" -ForegroundColor Red

        $FinalStatus |
            ForEach-Object {
                Write-Host "  $_"
            }

        throw (
            "Stage 4.1 delivery did not finish with a clean local main."
        )
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 17. FINAL REPORT
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "[DONE] STAGE 4.1 REPOSITORY BINDING IS IN MAIN" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "[STATE] Repository:      $Repository"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Reviewed base:   $ExpectedBaseHead"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Patch commit:    $ExpectedPatchCommit"
    Write-Host "[STATE] PR:              #$PrNumber $PrUrl"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Merge commit:    $MergeSha"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Current main:    $LocalMain"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Local == origin: YES"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Working tree:    CLEAN"
    Write-Host "[STATE] Exact-SHA CI:    GREEN"
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "[STATE] Recent main history:" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    Invoke-GitSafe `
        -Arguments @(
            "log",
            "--oneline",
            "--decorate",
            "-12"
        )

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host (
        "[NEXT] Stage 4.1 is finished. " +
        "Proceed to the dedicated GitHub Gauntlet/live multi-worker proof."
    ) -ForegroundColor Green
}
Why this is the right correction

The important change is that the runner now treats these as two different identities:

$ExpectedBaseHead
    = fbe193f...

$ExpectedPatchCommit
    = b6f21af...

The old script incorrectly continued demanding:

HEAD == reviewed base

even after it itself had made the commit. Your failure was therefore the runner protecting against something that had already happened legitimately. 

This replacement instead proves:

HEAD == exact known patch commit
patch parent == exact reviewed base
patch files == exact reviewed eight files
remote branch == exact patch commit
main == reviewed base before merge
PR head == exact patch commit
CI == green on exact patch commit
main still == reviewed base immediately before merge
merge == exact patch commit
origin/main contains patch
local main == origin/main

That is stronger than simply changing $ExpectedBaseHead to b6f21af..., which would have muddled the distinction between the reviewed integration base and the reviewed patch commit.

Also, I deliberately removed the local pre-commit stress loop from this continuation runner. We are past that stage. Re-running the flaky Windows pre-commit fixture again would add no integrity; the next legitimate gate is GitHub CI on the exact committed SHA.

Run this block as-is. The first meaningful output should be:

[PASS] Local HEAD is exact Stage 4.1 commit
[PASS] Working tree is clean
...
[PASS] origin/stage4-repository-binding-safety == b6f21af...
[WORK] Creating Stage 4.1 pull request

Then it should finally get us beyond the commit we already made and into PR/CI/merge.

That is nearly ideal context for me.

There is one important rule I would add

Historical context should never become repository authority.

We should explicitly say this in README.md:

Historical-Context-Sessions records rationale and operator history.

It is NOT authoritative for current repository state.

When historical context disagrees with:
- Git
- current main
- TaskGraph
- GitHub Issue workflow state
- committed policy/configuration
- current tests/evidence

the current deterministic repository state wins.

That protects us from exactly the danger that giant old transcripts create: an agent sees something that was true six hours ago and treats it as current fact.

This is also consistent with the architecture we've been building: conversation explains why; Git proves what.

I would be careful about committing raw sessions

The raw transcripts are useful, but before putting them in Git I would watch for:

API keys or authentication material
GitHub tokens
personal information
unrelated personal conversations
machine-specific secrets
enormous generated logs
duplicated terminal output

Our Task Orchestrator conversations are mostly technical, so they are pretty good candidates. But don't blindly export your entire ChatGPT history into the repository.

And huge raw transcripts will make Git grow. Text compresses well, but continually editing giant transcript files is ugly. Treat them as immutable snapshots: one file per completed session, never continually append to one 20-MB monster.

I'd also teach every agent where to look

Near the top of AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md, we could eventually add something as simple as:

## Project continuation context

For ongoing Task Orchestrator work, read:

Docs/AI-Pipeline/Historical-Context-Sessions/CURRENT_CONTEXT.md

Use referenced historical session documents only when additional
rationale is needed.

Historical session documents are advisory history, not repository
authority. Always verify current state from Git, TaskGraph, GitHub,
and committed configuration.

Then you could open a brand-new Claude, Codex, or ChatGPT session and say:

Continue the Task Orchestrator. Read the project context first.

And we'd have a fighting chance of actually starting where we left off rather than reconstructing two weeks of decisions.

So yes: I think you should do this.

I would just make it a two-layer system:

CURRENT_CONTEXT.md + concise session handoffs = working memory

raw/ transcripts = long-term historical memory

That is substantially better than relying on either giant chat contexts or my memory alone.

What this milestone means

Stage 4.1 repository binding / cross-repo safety
        ↓
LIVE MULTI-WORKER GAUNTLET

And current fresh_dispatch.py explicitly describes the design target: multiple PowerShell windows are independent synchronous workers; Stage 4 re-plans after ordinary contention, while operational failures remain terminal.

I would not add Stage 5/decomposition or more orchestration features now. We need to prove the thing works under real concurrency.

The next proof

We should create a dedicated private GitHub Gauntlet repository based on this exact main:

305930b5e3323a3f9533b6f5973df6a63685a5be

Not test this destructively against the real NoSafeCircle task queue.

The Gauntlet should contain synthetic tasks designed to prove these cases:

Test	Expected result
Two workers want same task	One wins; loser takes other work
Two tasks share exclusive resource	Only one can start
Two disjoint tasks	Both run concurrently
5–10 workers launched together	Safe tasks spread across workers
All remaining work conflicts	Worker returns no_safe_work
Ordinary claim race	Re-plan, not pipeline failure
Git/Issue operational failure	Stop; do not treat as contention
Durable agent-ready Issue exists	Resume it before fresh work
Wrong repository/task checkout	Fail closed
Cross-repository GitHub operation	Fail closed
Claims handed to Issue authority	Ephemeral refs released correctly
Worker crash/stale state	Durable authority remains understandable

The most important proof is not merely “no two workers got the same task.”

It is:

Launch N identical generic workers with no task IDs and watch them independently converge on the maximum set of mutually safe work.

Something like:

Window 1 → GAUNTLET-001 [player]
Window 2 → loses player conflict → GAUNTLET-004 [audio]
Window 3 → GAUNTLET-003 [UI]
Window 4 → loses GAUNTLET-001 → GAUNTLET-006 [enemy]
Window 5 → no safe work

with no human assigning any worker.

Run this complete block
& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE = "1"

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\OrchestratorGauntletBuilder"

    $SourceBranch =
        "orchestrator-gauntlet-v2-stage4-sync"

    $TargetBranch =
        "orchestrator-gauntlet-v2-stage4-1-sync"

    $ExpectedGauntletHead =
        "924d5d55c5bf32b98118bd57e9674b3eb0f4894a"

    $ExpectedProductionMain =
        "305930b5e3323a3f9533b6f5973df6a63685a5be"

    $ExpectedStage4Base =
        "fbe193f9578f02110005c78a72f7ef0d6a7fff06"

    # ============================================================
    # SAFE NATIVE COMMAND HELPERS
    # ============================================================

    function Invoke-GitSafe {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments,

            [int[]]$AllowedExitCodes = @(0),

            [switch]$Capture
        )

        $OldPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

        try {
            $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

            if ($Capture) {
                $Output = @(
                    & git @Arguments 2>&1 |
                    ForEach-Object {
                        [string]$_
                    }
                )
            }
            else {
                & git @Arguments 2>&1 |
                    ForEach-Object {
                        Write-Host ([string]$_)
                    }

                $Output = @()
            }

            $Code = $LASTEXITCODE
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $OldPreference
        }

        if ($AllowedExitCodes -notcontains $Code) {
            throw (
                "git " +
                ($Arguments -join " ") +
                " failed with exit code $Code."
            )
        }

        return [pscustomobject]@{
            ExitCode = $Code
            Output   = $Output
        }
    }

    function Get-GitText {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments
        )

        return (
            (
                Invoke-GitSafe `
                    -Arguments $Arguments `
                    -Capture
            ).Output -join "`n"
        ).Trim()
    }

    function Invoke-PythonChecked {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string]$Name,

            [Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]
            [string[]]$Arguments
        )

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] $Name" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $OldPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

        try {
            $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

            & python @Arguments 2>&1 |
                ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host ([string]$_)
                }

            $Code = $LASTEXITCODE
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $OldPreference
        }

        if ($Code -ne 0) {
            throw "$Name failed with exit code $Code."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] $Name" -ForegroundColor Green
    }

    function Invoke-Stage4ContentionChecked {

        $TestPath =
            "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/contention_retry_smoke_test.py"

        for ($Attempt = 1; $Attempt -le 5; $Attempt++) {

            Write-Host ""
            Write-Host (
                "[TEST] Stage 4 contention retry " +
                "attempt $Attempt/5"
            ) -ForegroundColor Cyan

            $AttemptLog = Join-Path $env:TEMP (
                "nsc-gauntlet-stage4-1-contention-" +
                (Get-Date -Format "yyyyMMdd-HHmmssfff") +
                ".txt"
            )

            $OldPreference = $ErrorActionPreference

            try {
                $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

                & python $TestPath 2>&1 |
                    Tee-Object -FilePath $AttemptLog |
                    ForEach-Object {
                        Write-Host ([string]$_)
                    }

                $Code = $LASTEXITCODE
            }
            finally {
                $ErrorActionPreference = $OldPreference
            }

            if ($Code -eq 0) {
                Write-Host (
                    "[PASS] Stage 4 contention retry"
                ) -ForegroundColor Green

                return
            }

            $Text = Get-Content `
                -LiteralPath $AttemptLog `
                -Raw

            $Normalized = [regex]::Replace(
                $Text,
                "\s+",
                " "
            )

            $Fixture =
                $Normalized -match "nsc-stage4-stress-" -and
                $Normalized -match "remote\.git"

            $PermissionDenied =
                $Normalized -match "Permission denied"

            $CorrectClassification =
                $Normalized -match "claim_operational_error"

            $KnownRefLockFlake =
                $Normalized -match (
                    "cannot lock ref " +
                    "'refs/nsc/claims/.*?\.lock'.*?" +
                    "Permission denied"
                )

            $KnownObjectStoreFlake =
                $Normalized -match (
                    "unable to migrate objects " +
                    "to permanent storage"
                )

            $ExactWindowsFixtureFlake =
                $Fixture -and
                $PermissionDenied -and
                $CorrectClassification -and
                (
                    $KnownRefLockFlake -or
                    $KnownObjectStoreFlake
                )

            if (
                $ExactWindowsFixtureFlake -and
                $Attempt -lt 5
            ) {
                Write-Host ""
                Write-Host (
                    "[RETRY] Known Windows temporary bare-repo " +
                    "filesystem fixture failure."
                ) -ForegroundColor Yellow

                Write-Host (
                    "[CHECK] Production classified it correctly as " +
                    "claim_operational_error."
                ) -ForegroundColor Yellow

                Start-Sleep -Seconds 2
                continue
            }

            Write-Host ""
            Write-Host (
                "[BLOCKED] Real Stage 4 contention test failure."
            ) -ForegroundColor Red

            Write-Host "[LOG] $AttemptLog"

            throw "Stage 4 contention regression failed."
        }

        throw "Stage 4 contention suite never completed cleanly."
    }

    Set-Location $Root

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host " GAUNTLET V2 — SYNC WITH PRODUCTION STAGE 4.1" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    Write-Host "[CHECKPOINT]" -ForegroundColor Yellow
    Write-Host "Repo:        $Root"
    Write-Host "Source:      $SourceBranch"

    Write-Host "GitHub write: DISABLED"

    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE = "1"

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\NoSafeCircle"
    $ExpectedMain = "f1ad90f6a4cee16b14f8dc313c8831fc7743d587"
    $Branch = "fix/historical-context-scene-policy"

    $PolicyPath =
        "Pipeline/TaskGraph/scene_path_policy.py"

    $TestPath =
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/scene_path_contract_migration_smoke_test.py"

    Set-Location $Root

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host " FIX HISTORICAL RAW CONTEXT SCENE-PATH POLICY" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
    Write-Host ""

    # ============================================================
    # 1. EXACT CLEAN MAIN
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host "[VERIFY] Updating exact production main" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch main
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not switch to main."
    }

    git pull --ff-only origin main
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not fast-forward main."
    }

    $Head = (git rev-parse HEAD).Trim()

    if ($Head -ne $ExpectedMain) {
        throw @"
Production main moved again.

Expected:
$ExpectedMain

Current:
$Head

Stop so the new head can be reviewed.
"@
    }

    $Status = @(
        git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all
    )

    if ($Status.Count -ne 0) {
        git status --short --untracked-files=all
        throw "Production main is not clean."
    }

    Write-Host "[PASS] Exact clean production main: $Head" -ForegroundColor Green

    # ============================================================
    # 2. CREATE FIX BRANCH
    # ============================================================

    $ExistingBranch = git show-ref `
        --verify `
        --quiet `
        "refs/heads/$Branch"

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) {
        throw "Local branch already exists: $Branch"
    }

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[WORK] Creating $Branch" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    git switch -c $Branch
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
        throw "Could not create fix branch."
    }

    # ============================================================
    # 3. PATCH SCENE POLICY
    # ============================================================

    Write-Host ""
    Write-Host "[EDIT] Excluding immutable raw context transcripts" -ForegroundColor Cyan

    $Policy = Get-Content `
        -LiteralPath $PolicyPath `
        -Raw `
        -Encoding UTF8

    $PolicyNeedle = @'
    "Pipeline/TaskGraph/migrations/",
)
'@

    $PolicyReplacement = @'
    "Pipeline/TaskGraph/migrations/",
    "Docs/AI-Pipeline/Historical-Context-Sessions/raw/",
)
'@

    if ($Policy -notlike "*$PolicyNeedle*") {
        throw "Could not find the expected immutable-prefix block in scene_path_policy.py."
    }

    $Policy = $Policy.Replace(
        $PolicyNeedle,
        $PolicyReplacement
    )

    [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText(

**You:**

Can we set a standard in the repository on how to not have commands that break? Maybe we need to upgrade the doc because we do have a command example but maybe not enough dos and donts?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. I think we have enough evidence now that operator-command reliability needs to be a repository standard, not a collection of examples.

The repository already has a real native-command helper that solves two of our recurring failures: Windows PowerShell 5.1 treating harmless native stderr as terminating errors, and CRLF payloads breaking Linux commands inside Docker. But AGENTS.md currently does not tell agents that they must follow a command-construction standard before giving you operational PowerShell. The Game Task Agent runbook gives working examples, but not enough rules for constructing new ones safely.

I would create one canonical document:

Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md

and add a short mandatory pointer in AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md:

Before producing or executing PowerShell, Git, Docker, GitHub CLI, Python, or other operator commands, read and follow Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md.

The new document should contain hard rules, not just examples.

The rules I would put in it
Assume Windows PowerShell 5.1 unless explicitly told otherwise. No Bash stdin redirection such as < file, no PowerShell-7-only syntax.
Give complete commands. A multi-step operation should normally be one & { ... } block with all required variables defined inside it. Do not rely on state from an earlier pasted snippet.
Native executables are not PowerShell cmdlets. For repository scripts, use Invoke-NscNativeCommand where available. Otherwise temporarily set $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue", capture $LASTEXITCODE, restore the preference, and decide success from the actual exit code. This is exactly what our helper already does.
Never send raw Windows multiline text across a Linux boundary. Normalize every multiline argument with:
.Replace("`r`n","`n").Replace("`r","`n")
before passing it to Docker/Linux. The repository helper already codifies this.
Do not patch files by exact multiline string matching when newline format matters. This is the bug we just hit. Prefer, in order:
structured editing;
line-based insertion;
a small Python editing script with explicit assertions;
regex with an exact expected match count.
Avoid:
$Text.Replace($HugeMultilineHereString, ...)
unless both sides are normalized first and the match count is proven.
Every edit must prove its anchor before mutation.
expected exactly 1 match
found 0 → stop
found >1 → stop
Never “best effort” patch a repository file.
Every mutating runner must be resume-safe. It must distinguish:
expected base SHA
resulting commit SHA
pushed branch SHA
merged SHA
and recognize already-completed stages. This would have prevented the Stage 4.1 runner from rejecting the commit it had already created.
Every mutation gets preconditions and postconditions.
Before:
expected repository;
expected branch;
expected HEAD/base;
clean tree;
expected changed-file scope.
After:
expected resulting HEAD;
expected file scope;
remote identity where relevant;
clean tree.
Never use git add -A for reviewed/bounded changes. Stage exact known paths and verify the staged list.
Never silently recover with destructive Git. No reset --hard, clean -fd, forced branch movement, or force push unless the command is explicitly a reviewed recovery operation.
Do not hardcode repository authority. Resolve repository identity from the checkout/origin and assert it matches expectations. Stage 4.1 now enforces this in production.
Commands that depend on an exact SHA must stop safely if it moved. But the error must identify whether the SHA was:
base;
candidate;
remote branch;
merged main.
Avoid a generic $ExpectedHead when multiple identities exist.
Long-running commands must log progress. Print [START], [READ], [WORK], [TEST], [PASS], [BLOCKED], [DONE], and heartbeat/progress for agent calls.
A command should never require the operator to infer whether it succeeded. Its final state should say what changed:
[DONE]
Branch:
Commit:
PR:
Current main:
Working tree:
Next action:
Test the command-construction boundary itself. We already have native_command_smoke_test.ps1. We should add a small operator_command_policy_smoke_test only where a deterministic check is practical—not try to lint every PowerShell stylistic choice.
I would also add a “DO / DON'T” section

For example:

DO
✓ wrap a complete operation in & { ... }
✓ define variables inside the block
✓ verify branch/HEAD/tree before mutation
✓ inspect $LASTEXITCODE for native programs
✓ normalize CRLF before Linux/Docker
✓ verify exact match counts before automated text edits
✓ stage exact files
✓ make runners resumable

DON'T
✗ use Bash < redirection in Windows PowerShell
✗ rely on harmless stderr not becoming a PowerShell error
✗ use exact multiline .Replace() across unknown line endings
✗ assume HEAD still means the pre-commit base after committing
✗ use git add -A for bounded reviewed work
✗ silently reset/clean/force-push
✗ give half a command that depends on variables from a previous paste
✗ let a 120-turn agent repeatedly execute an action that made no progress
And a canonical command skeleton

This is probably the missing piece. Every agent should start from something like:

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\NoSafeCircle"
    $ExpectedBranch = "..."
    $ExpectedHead = "..."

    Set-Location $Root

    # PRECONDITIONS
    # repository / branch / head / clean tree

    # SAFE NATIVE COMMAND HELPERS
    # or dot-source NativeCommand.ps1

    # WORK
    # bounded mutation

    # VALIDATION
    # tests / exact scope / whitespace

    # POSTCONDITIONS
    # resulting head / remote state / clean tree

    # FINAL REPORT
}

The examples in runbooks can then remain task-specific, while this document defines how all future examples must be constructed.

I would not bury all of this in GAME_TASK_AGENT_RUNBOOK.md. That document is about operating the Game Task Agent. This is broader: it governs commands generated by ChatGPT, Claude, Codex, pipeline maintenance scripts, Gauntlet setup, recovery runners, and probably future decomposition work.

So my recommendation is:

Create OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md, reference it mandatorily from AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md, and add one or two targeted regression tests for the rules that are actually machine-testable.

And yes, the two failures from tonight should go directly into it as examples:

Bad: newline-sensitive multiline .Replace() → the historical-context policy edit.
Bad: runner assumes HEAD == base after it already committed → Stage 4.1 delivery.
Good: exact state model + resume-safe runner.
Good: line-based editing with exact anchor counts.

That turns the pain we just paid for into repository knowledge so the next agent doesn't make us pay for it again.

**You:**

Can you look through the 2 files I sent you for context and see all of the command failures and see if there are other examples you might not have thought of?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. I went back through both large context files specifically looking for operator-command failures and near-failures, rather than architecture or pipeline-code defects. There are quite a few more patterns than the ones I initially remembered. The history shows that command reliability has become a genuine engineering concern in this project, not just “be more careful with PowerShell.”  

The biggest conclusion is that OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md should cover seven or eight distinct failure classes, and we should enforce some of them mechanically.

Failure class	Actual thing that happened	Standard we should establish
PowerShell 5.1 parser incompatibility	I gave you Bash-style < $PromptPath; PowerShell rejected the entire block with RedirectionNotSupported.	Assume Windows PowerShell 5.1. No Bash redirection or PowerShell-7-only syntax.
PowerShell source encoding	A generated .ps1 saved as UTF-8 without BOM contained Unicode punctuation; Windows PowerShell 5.1 decoded it incorrectly and punctuation became syntax.	Executable PS5 operator scripts should be ASCII-safe or UTF-8 BOM, and generated scripts should receive a parser preflight.
Variable interpolation parsing	"[PASS] PR #$PrNumber: $PrUrl" caused a parse problem because : directly followed the variable.	Use $($Variable) or ${Variable} next to punctuation that PowerShell can interpret as part of a variable reference.
Incomplete interactive syntax	At one point elseif/else were sent separately from their if block and PowerShell treated them as commands.	Compound PowerShell constructs must always be delivered as one syntactically complete block.
$PSScriptRoot initialization timing	A parameter default derived from $PSScriptRoot became \Stage0-Repair-Prompt.txt rather than the intended script-relative path.	Do not use $PSScriptRoot to calculate param() defaults. Resolve script-relative defaults after param().
Native stderr vs PowerShell errors	Normal Docker/Git stderr became NativeCommandError under $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop".	Native success/failure comes from $LASTEXITCODE, not whether stderr was emitted. Use the repository native-command wrapper.
2>$null as false safety	A Git LF/CRLF warning still interfered with Stage 4 logic despite stderr suppression attempts.	Do not depend on redirection as the native-error policy. Capture exit status deliberately.
stderr contaminating captured data	A Git line-ending warning got mixed into an array intended to contain filenames; .Count became nonzero and the runner falsely concluded claim_refs.py had changed.	Never use captured native text as a boolean when stderr can be mixed in. For predicates use commands like git diff --quiet and interpret documented exit codes.
Expected nonzero exits	git diff --quiet, git show-ref --quiet, and git merge-base --is-ancestor legitimately return 1 for “false,” but generic wrappers can interpret that as failure.	Every native call must know its accepted exit-code set, e.g. @(0,1) for predicates.
Windows → Linux CRLF boundary	Multiline PowerShell arguments carried CR characters into Bash/Docker and caused invalid tokens/options.	Normalize every multiline native payload to LF before crossing into Linux. NativeCommand.ps1 already implements this correctly.
Container CRLF false diffs	Claude's Linux checkout reported roughly 911 modified files, while Windows Git showed only the intended ~28 changes.	For bind-mounted Windows repositories, host Git is final changed-file authority. Container diff results must account for CRLF.
Console wrapping corrupting pattern matching	A known Git error appeared with line wrapping inside phrases—and even inside NSC-801.lock—so the wrapper failed to recognize the known Windows fixture problem.	Normalize captured diagnostic whitespace before textual matching. Better yet, prefer typed/structured error classifications over matching display text.
Brittle text editing	We repeatedly relied on exact text shapes and newline assumptions. Our current historical-context edit failure is another example of the same family.	Repository mutation must use structured, line-based, or exact-count regex editing. Never do an unchecked large multiline .Replace().
Deep Windows temp paths	A validation clone failed with Filename too long.	Keep temp/checkouts deliberately short. For Git clones requiring long paths, enable core.longpaths before checkout.
8.3 vs expanded paths	%TEMP% produced VINCEN~1 while another API returned VincentLiguori; substring arithmetic produced paths like validation-repository\itory\Pipeline\....	Never derive relative paths with string slicing. Use proper path APIs / Git-relative paths.
Absolute-vs-relative confusion	Validation code combined repository roots with values that were already absolute, producing malformed paths.	Treat absolute paths and repository-relative paths as different data types/concepts. Validate before joining.
Dubious ownership from local clones	A validation clone sourced from a local Git repository triggered Git's dubious ownership protection.	Disposable verification clones should normally use the trusted origin remote. Do not “fix” this with global safe.directory.
Stale current working directory	A PowerShell window remained in the old, moved checkout. Even docker.exe couldn't launch because Windows said the working directory was invalid.	Every standalone runner establishes and validates its own root with Test-Path + Set-Location; never trust the shell's inherited CWD.
State-blind reruns	Stage 4.1's original delivery runner expected HEAD == reviewed base even though an earlier attempt had already successfully created the reviewed commit.	Mutating runners must be resume-safe and distinguish base SHA, patch SHA, pushed SHA, PR head, merge SHA and current main.
Huge unbounded output	A broad git grep returned so much historical material that you stopped scrolling.	Output volume is part of command correctness. Prefer filenames, counts, scoped roots and exclusions. Save detailed output to a log.
Silent long-running commands	Fable/Claude looked frozen and required a second PowerShell window with docker stats to establish whether work was happening.	Anything potentially long-running must stream progress or heartbeat and identify its log/output location.
Abandoned containers	An interactive bash → claude container stayed alive for about ten days, retaining gigabytes of RAM until we discovered it.	Noninteractive automation must launch the actual bounded command, not an open-ended shell. Long-running containers require lifecycle/timeout/heartbeat rules.
Ambiguous cleanup behavior	After docker stop, docker rm reported No such container because --rm had already removed it.	Runners should explain expected lifecycle behavior so a successful cleanup is not mistaken for another failure.

Those examples aren't hypothetical. The context records the Bash-redirection parser failure, the moved/deleted working-directory failure, the abandoned Docker/Claude process, the giant grep output, and the host-vs-container CRLF mismatch directly.  The earlier pipeline history also contains the PowerShell encoding, validation-clone/path, and native-process failures that led us to harden the launcher/runtime approach. 

One rule I hadn't appreciated enough: classify where a command failed

I think this needs an entire section in the standard. We have repeatedly treated “the command failed” as one condition, but recovery depends completely on the stage:

PARSE FAILURE
    PowerShell never executed the block.
    Safe to correct syntax and rerun.

PRECONDITION FAILURE
    Runner deliberately stopped before mutation.
    Recheck state, then rerun or revise.

RUNTIME FAILURE BEFORE MUTATION
    A tool ran but authoritative state was not changed.
    Diagnose operational error.

PARTIAL MUTATION
    Commit / push / Issue / PR may already exist.
    DO NOT blindly rerun.
    Observe current state and resume from what succeeded.

EXPECTED PREDICATE FALSE
    Exit code 1 may simply mean "not present" or "not ancestor."
    This is data, not an operational error.

TRANSIENT OPERATIONAL FAILURE
    Filesystem / temp Git / provider problem.
    Retry only when positively classified as transient.

That one distinction would have prevented a lot of Stage 4.1 confusion. The earlier delivery runner saw a changed HEAD and treated it as “something went wrong,” when the thing that changed HEAD was its own successful prior commit step.

There are also some dangerous patterns that happened to work

The histories contain a few commands I wouldn't call failures, but I don't want future agents copying them. Examples include git add -A during a bounded reviewed patch, using a raw changed-file count as the scope guarantee, relying heavily on trailing PowerShell backticks, and reproducing huge commands instead of creating reusable checked-in runners once they stabilize.

So the standard should distinguish:

PROHIBITED
Known to have failed us.

DISCOURAGED
Worked, but has a safer alternative.

REQUIRED
Repository-approved construction pattern.
I would strengthen the implementation beyond documentation

After reading the histories, I don't think a Markdown file alone is enough. We already have the beginning of the right solution in Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/NativeCommand.ps1: it normalizes CRLF and correctly handles native stderr separately from exit status.

I would make the repository standard consist of three pieces:

Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md
        ↓
human + agent construction rules

AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md
        ↓
mandatory read-before-producing-operator-commands pointer

small deterministic checks
        ↓
catch the expensive mistakes before Vincent pastes them

The small checks should catch only high-confidence footguns, for example:

Parse repository operator .ps1 files with the PowerShell parser.

Reject Bash-style:
    < $SomePath

Ensure AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md reference the standard.

Regression-test Invoke-NscNativeCommand.

Possibly flag $PSScriptRoot inside param() defaults.

Possibly flag obvious native-command captures performed while
$ErrorActionPreference = "Stop".

I would not build a giant PowerShell linter. That would be repeating our tendency to turn every problem into another framework.

The canonical command template should also change

My earlier template was missing a recovery model. I now think every substantial mutating operator runner should visibly follow:

IDENTITY
    repo
    expected branch
    expected starting authority

PREFLIGHT
    path exists
    correct repo
    clean/expected tree
    required tools

OBSERVE CURRENT STATE
    determine whether earlier stages already happened

WORK
    perform exactly the next missing mutation

VALIDATE
    exact changed files
    tests
    hashes / SHAs / remote state

POSTCONDITIONS
    prove resulting state

FINAL REPORT
    what happened
    what did not happen
    exact current SHA/branch
    whether rerunning is safe
    next action

The important addition is OBSERVE CURRENT STATE before WORK. That turns a script from “instructions that work once” into a resume-safe operation.

So yes: going through those two files materially changed my recommendation. The document needs more than a few Dos and Don'ts. We have enough historical failures now to build a pretty strong, evidence-based operator command contract.

I think this is worth implementing before we go much farther with the live Gauntlet, because we're about to intentionally launch multiple workers and do more complicated Git/GitHub operations. Reducing operator-command errors before that will pay for itself quickly.

Windows PowerShell 5.1 is the compatibility baseline.
It discourages continuation backticks when safer forms exist.
Generated .ps1 files require parser preflight.
Native processes go through NativeCommand.ps1, so stderr is not confused with command failure.
Git predicates explicitly allow meaningful exit codes like 0/1.

Long-running provider work has explicit visibility/lifecycle requirements.

What is now in the standard

This is much stronger than a list of tips. It defines REQUIRED / PROHIBITED / DISCOURAGED behavior and explicitly covers the failures from the historical sessions.

For example, it now mandates Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatibility, prohibits Bash syntax in PowerShell, discourages fragile continuation backticks, requires complete compound syntax, prohibits $PSScriptRoot-dependent param() defaults, requires explicit statement boundaries so commands cannot accidentally concatenate, and requires parser preflight for generated .ps1 files.

It also covers the other major families we found:

Native command handling
stderr is not failure;
$LASTEXITCODE is authoritative;
predicate commands declare allowed exit codes;
don't use captured output counts when warnings can contaminate them.
Cross-Windows/Linux boundaries
normalize CRLF before Docker/Linux;
host Git is authority for Windows bind-mounted checkout changes;
structured CLI output should use UTF-8.
Text editing
no unchecked multiline .Replace();
structured → line-based → exact-count scripted editing preference;
exactly 0 / 1 / >1 anchor behavior;
verify exact changed paths immediately.
Git safety
exact-path staging;
no git add . / git add -A for bounded work;
distinguish ReviewedBase, PatchCommit, RemoteBranchHead, PullRequestHead, MergeCommit, CurrentMain;
observe state before resuming;
no blind reset/clean/force-push recovery.
Paths
path APIs instead of string slicing;
distinguish absolute and relative paths;
deliberately short Windows clone/temp roots;
no global safe.directory workaround.
Large prompts and payloads
long/quote-heavy prompts, JSON, patches, etc. go into UTF-8 files rather than giant command-line arguments;
specifically accounts for command-line length, quoting and chat transport failures.
Provider runs
deterministic setup and expensive provider execution should normally be separate phases;
long work needs progress/heartbeat;
bounded automation must not leave open-ended interactive containers behind.
Output
avoid enormous repository-wide dumps;
save verbose results to logs;
every successful substantial command ends with explicit [DONE] / [STATE] / [NEXT].
The part I think matters most

The standards now formally define six different failure classes:

Parse failure
Precondition failure
Expected predicate false
Runtime failure before durable mutation
Partial mutation
Transient operational failure

And each has different recovery rules.

That means future agents aren't allowed to treat:

"the command didn't reach [DONE]"

as equivalent to:

"nothing happened"

That was one of the most expensive recurring mistakes in our history.

There is also now a full agent pre-handoff checklist. Before giving you a substantial paste-ready command, an agent is supposed to review syntax, state ownership, resume safety, native calls, editing behavior, Git scope, paths, Linux boundaries, large payload handling, progress visibility, and final/recovery reporting.

I also kept the proposed automation enforcement deliberately small. The standards explicitly warn not to create a giant command-style linter. The machine-checkable subset should focus on things like PowerShell parser checks, native-command regression tests, obvious Bash syntax, dangerous $PSScriptRoot defaults, and LF/CRLF edit-helper testing.

OPERATOR_FILE_HANDOFF_AND_DOWNLOADS.md
    Where prompts/logs/artifacts go and how humans receive them

    Existing implementation of safe native-process behavior

That feels like the right separation of concerns.
