**You:**

okay can you give me a new command for pr 100?

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. PR #100 is still open as a draft at head 7b68f887f56d852b6787b7159a902fec1716b84a, while main is now 920bd7ca18e0c39cc81145a546956a8f804ef963.

This command is resume-safe. It updates the PR #100 branch with the repaired main, runs the relevant local checks, verifies the PR still contains exactly its intended six files, and pushes it. It does not merge PR #100 and does not touch the Gauntlet.

& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"
    $env:PYTHONUTF8 = "1"
    $env:PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE = "1"

    # ============================================================
    # IDENTITY
    # ============================================================

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\NoSafeCircle"
    $Repository = "cathode26/NoSafeCircle"
    $PrNumber = 100
    $PrBranch = "docs/operator-command-template"

    $OriginalPrHead = "7b68f887f56d852b6787b7159a902fec1716b84a"
    $ExpectedMain = "920bd7ca18e0c39cc81145a546956a8f804ef963"

    $SyncMessage = "merge: update PR 100 with historical policy fix"

    $ExpectedPrPaths = @(
        ".github/workflows/task-review-agent-supervisor.yml",
        "AGENTS.md",
        "CLAUDE.md",
        "Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md",
        "Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_COMMAND_TEMPLATE.md",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/operator_command_policy_smoke_test.ps1"
    ) | Sort-Object

    $CurrentPhase = "identity"
    $MergeCreated = $false
    $PushCompleted = $false
    $NativeHelperLoaded = $false

    function Invoke-CheckedNative {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
            [string]$FilePath,

            [Parameter()]
            [string[]]$ArgumentList = @(),

            [Parameter()]
            [int[]]$AllowedExitCodes = @(0),

            [switch]$StreamOutput
        )

        $Result = Invoke-NscNativeCommand `
            -FilePath $FilePath `
            -ArgumentList $ArgumentList `
            -StreamOutput:$StreamOutput

        if ($AllowedExitCodes -notcontains $Result.ExitCode) {
            if (-not $StreamOutput) {
                $Result.Output | ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host $_
                }
            }

            throw (
                "$FilePath failed with exit code " +
                $Result.ExitCode +
                "."
            )
        }

        return $Result
    }

    function Get-NativeText {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
            [string]$FilePath,

            [Parameter()]
            [string[]]$ArgumentList = @()
        )

        $Result = Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath $FilePath `
            -ArgumentList $ArgumentList

        return (($Result.Output -join "`n").Trim())
    }

    function Test-ExpectedSyncMerge {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
            [string]$Sha
        )

        $ParentsText = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "show",
                "-s",
                "--format=%P",
                $Sha
            )

        $Parents = @(
            $ParentsText -split "\s+" |
                Where-Object {
                    -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
                }
        )

        if ($Parents.Count -ne 2) {
            return $false
        }

        if ($Parents[0] -ne $OriginalPrHead) {
            return $false
        }

        if ($Parents[1] -ne $ExpectedMain) {
            return $false
        }

        $Subject = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "show",
                "-s",
                "--format=%s",
                $Sha
            )

        return $Subject -eq $SyncMessage
    }

    function Show-RecoveryState {
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "[RECOVERY] PR 100 UPDATE STOPPED" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Phase:         $CurrentPhase"
        Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Merge created: $MergeCreated"
        Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Push complete: $PushCompleted"

        if (-not $NativeHelperLoaded) {
            return
        }

        try {
            $Branch = Get-NativeText `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "branch",
                    "--show-current"
                )

            $Head = Get-NativeText `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "rev-parse",
                    "HEAD"
                )

            Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Branch: $Branch"
            Write-Host "[RECOVERY] HEAD:   $Head"

            $Status = Get-NativeText `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "status",
                    "--porcelain=v1",
                    "--untracked-files=all"
                )

            if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($Status)) {
                Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Tree:   CLEAN"
            }
            else {
                Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Tree:   NOT CLEAN"
                Write-Host $Status
            }
        }
        catch {
            Write-Host (
                "[RECOVERY] State inspection failed: " +
                $_.Exception.Message
            )
        }
    }

    try {
        # ============================================================
        # PREFLIGHT
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "preflight"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host " UPDATE PR #100 ONTO REPAIRED MAIN" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host ""

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Root -PathType Container)) {
            throw "Repository root is missing: $Root"
        }

        Set-Location $Root

        $NativeHelper = Join-Path `
            $Root `
            "Pipeline\TaskReviewAgent\NativeCommand.ps1"

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $NativeHelper -PathType Leaf)) {
            throw "NativeCommand.ps1 is missing."
        }

        . $NativeHelper
        $NativeHelperLoaded = $true

        $ActualRoot = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "--show-toplevel"
            )

        if (
            [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($ActualRoot).TrimEnd("\") -ne
            [System.IO.Path]::GetFullPath($Root).TrimEnd("\")
        ) {
            throw "This is not the expected controller repository."
        }

        $Origin = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "remote",
                "get-url",
                "origin"
            )

        $AllowedOrigins = @(
            "https://github.com/cathode26/NoSafeCircle.git",
            "https://github.com/cathode26/NoSafeCircle"
        )

        if ($AllowedOrigins -notcontains $Origin) {
            throw "Unexpected origin: $Origin"
        }

        $InitialStatus = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($InitialStatus)) {
            Write-Host $InitialStatus
            throw "Working tree is not clean. Nothing was changed."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Correct repository"
        Write-Host "[PASS] Initial working tree clean"

        # ============================================================
        # REFRESH AUTHORITY
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "fetch"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[READ] Fetching current remote state" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "--prune"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        $RemoteMain = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/main"
            )

        if ($RemoteMain -ne $ExpectedMain) {
            throw (
                "main moved again.`nExpected: " +
                $ExpectedMain +
                "`nCurrent:  " +
                $RemoteMain
            )
        }

        $RemotePrHead = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/$PrBranch"
            )

        Write-Host "[STATE] Current main:      $RemoteMain"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Remote PR branch: $RemotePrHead"

        # ============================================================
        # VERIFY LIVE PR
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "verify-pr"

        $PrJson = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "gh" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "number,state,isDraft,headRefName,headRefOid,baseRefName,url"
            )

        $Pr = $PrJson | ConvertFrom-Json

        if ($Pr.number -ne $PrNumber) {
            throw "Resolved the wrong pull request."
        }

        if ($Pr.state -ne "OPEN") {
            throw "PR #100 is no longer open."
        }

        if ($Pr.baseRefName -ne "main") {
            throw "PR #100 no longer targets main."
        }

        if ($Pr.headRefName -ne $PrBranch) {
            throw "PR #100 now uses an unexpected head branch."
        }

        if ($Pr.headRefOid -ne $RemotePrHead) {
            throw "GitHub PR head and remote branch head disagree."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] PR #100 identity verified"
        Write-Host "[STATE] PR URL: $($Pr.url)"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Draft:  $($Pr.isDraft)"

        # ============================================================
        # CLASSIFY REMOTE PR STATE
        # ============================================================

        $RemoteAlreadySynced = $false

        if ($RemotePrHead -eq $OriginalPrHead) {
            Write-Host "[STATE] Remote PR still needs repaired main."
        }
        elseif (Test-ExpectedSyncMerge -Sha $RemotePrHead) {
            $RemoteAlreadySynced = $true
            Write-Host "[STATE] Remote PR already contains expected sync merge."
        }
        else {
            throw (
                "PR branch moved to an unrecognized commit: " +
                $RemotePrHead
            )
        }

        # ============================================================
        # SWITCH / CREATE LOCAL PR BRANCH
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "switch-branch"

        $LocalBranchProbe = Invoke-NscNativeCommand `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "show-ref",
                "--verify",
                "--quiet",
                "refs/heads/$PrBranch"
            )

        if (@(0, 1) -notcontains $LocalBranchProbe.ExitCode) {
            throw "Could not inspect local PR branch."
        }

        if ($LocalBranchProbe.ExitCode -eq 0) {
            Invoke-CheckedNative `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "switch",
                    $PrBranch
                ) `
                -StreamOutput | Out-Null
        }
        else {
            Invoke-CheckedNative `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "switch",
                    "-c",
                    $PrBranch,
                    "--track",
                    "origin/$PrBranch"
                ) `
                -StreamOutput | Out-Null
        }

        $BranchStatus = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($BranchStatus)) {
            throw "PR branch is not clean after switching."
        }

        $LocalHead = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "HEAD"
            )

        Write-Host "[STATE] Local PR HEAD: $LocalHead"

        # ============================================================
        # OBSERVE / RESUME
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "observe"

        if ($RemoteAlreadySynced) {
            if ($LocalHead -eq $RemotePrHead) {
                Write-Host "[STATE] Local and remote PR branch already synchronized."
            }
            elseif ($LocalHead -eq $OriginalPrHead) {
                Write-Host "[WORK] Fast-forwarding stale local PR branch"

                Invoke-CheckedNative `
                    -FilePath "git" `
                    -ArgumentList @(
                        "merge",
                        "--ff-only",
                        "origin/$PrBranch"
                    ) `
                    -StreamOutput | Out-Null

                $LocalHead = Get-NativeText `
                    -FilePath "git" `
                    -ArgumentList @(
                        "rev-parse",
                        "HEAD"
                    )

                if ($LocalHead -ne $RemotePrHead) {
                    throw "Local branch did not fast-forward to remote PR head."
                }
            }
            else {
                throw (
                    "Local PR branch has unexpected divergent state: " +
                    $LocalHead
                )
            }
        }
        else {
            if ($LocalHead -eq $OriginalPrHead) {
                # Normal fresh path.
            }
            elseif (Test-ExpectedSyncMerge -Sha $LocalHead) {
                Write-Host "[STATE] Expected merge already exists locally; push was not completed."
                $MergeCreated = $true
            }
            else {
                throw (
                    "Local PR branch contains an unrecognized commit: " +
                    $LocalHead
                )
            }
        }

        # ============================================================
        # MERGE REPAIRED MAIN WHEN STILL NEEDED
        # ============================================================

        if (
            -not $RemoteAlreadySynced -and
            -not $MergeCreated
        ) {
            $CurrentPhase = "merge-main"

            Write-Host ""
            Write-Host "[WORK] Merging repaired main into PR #100" -ForegroundColor Cyan

            $MergeResult = Invoke-NscNativeCommand `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "-c",
                    "user.name=No Safe Circle TaskReviewAgent",
                    "-c",
                    "user.email=task-review-agent@nosafecircle.invalid",
                    "merge",
                    "--no-ff",
                    "origin/main",
                    "-m",
                    $SyncMessage
                ) `
                -StreamOutput

            if ($MergeResult.ExitCode -ne 0) {
                throw (
                    "Merge failed. Do not reset or clean the checkout; " +
                    "send me the recovery output."
                )
            }

            $LocalHead = Get-NativeText `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "rev-parse",
                    "HEAD"
                )

            if (-not (Test-ExpectedSyncMerge -Sha $LocalHead)) {
                throw "Created merge commit does not have the expected identity."
            }

            $MergeCreated = $true

            Write-Host "[PASS] Expected sync merge created"
            Write-Host "[STATE] Synced PR HEAD: $LocalHead"
        }
        else {
            $LocalHead = Get-NativeText `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "rev-parse",
                    "HEAD"
                )
        }

        # ============================================================
        # EXACT PR SCOPE
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "scope"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[VERIFY] PR #100 exact changed-file scope" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $ChangedResult = Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "diff",
                "--name-only",
                "origin/main...HEAD"
            )

        $ActualPrPaths = @(
            $ChangedResult.Output |
                Where-Object {
                    -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
                } |
                Sort-Object
        )

        $ScopeDifference = @(
            Compare-Object `
                $ExpectedPrPaths `
                $ActualPrPaths
        )

        if ($ScopeDifference.Count -ne 0) {
            Write-Host "[EXPECTED]"
            $ExpectedPrPaths | ForEach-Object {
                Write-Host "  $_"
            }

            Write-Host "[ACTUAL]"
            $ActualPrPaths | ForEach-Object {
                Write-Host "  $_"
            }

            throw "PR #100 changed-file scope is no longer exact."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Exactly six intended PR files"

        # ============================================================
        # TARGETED LOCAL VALIDATION
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "validation"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] Operator command policy smoke test" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "powershell.exe" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "-NoProfile",
                "-ExecutionPolicy",
                "Bypass",
                "-File",
                "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/operator_command_policy_smoke_test.ps1"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] Operator command policy smoke test"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] Native stderr / CRLF smoke test" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "powershell.exe" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "-NoProfile",
                "-ExecutionPolicy",
                "Bypass",
                "-File",
                "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/native_command_smoke_test.ps1"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] Native command smoke test"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] Canonical checkout-root policy" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "python" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/checkout_root_policy_smoke_test.py"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] Checkout-root policy"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] Current TaskGraph validation" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "python" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "Pipeline/TaskGraph/taskcontrol.py",
                "validate"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] TaskGraph validation"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] CI split regression" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "python" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/ci_workflow_split_smoke_test.py"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] CI split regression"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] Python compile" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "python" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "-m",
                "compileall",
                "-q",
                "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent",
                "Pipeline/TaskGraph"
            ) | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] Python compile"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[TEST] PR whitespace" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "-c",
                "core.whitespace=cr-at-eol",
                "diff",
                "--check",
                "origin/main...HEAD"
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host "[PASS] PR whitespace"

        # ============================================================
        # FINAL CLEAN TREE BEFORE PUSH
        # ============================================================

        $PrePushStatus = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($PrePushStatus)) {
            Write-Host $PrePushStatus
            throw "Validation changed the working tree."
        }

        # ============================================================
        # PUSH ONLY WHEN NEEDED
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "push"

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "--prune"
            ) | Out-Null

        $RemotePrHead = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/$PrBranch"
            )

        if ($RemotePrHead -eq $LocalHead) {
            Write-Host "[STATE] Remote PR branch already has validated sync commit."
            $PushCompleted = $true
        }
        elseif ($RemotePrHead -eq $OriginalPrHead) {
            Write-Host ""
            Write-Host "[WORK] Pushing validated PR #100 update" -ForegroundColor Cyan

            Invoke-CheckedNative `
                -FilePath "git" `
                -ArgumentList @(
                    "push",
                    "origin",
                    "HEAD:refs/heads/$PrBranch"
                ) `
                -StreamOutput | Out-Null

            $PushCompleted = $true
        }
        else {
            throw (
                "Remote PR branch moved during validation. Current remote: " +
                $RemotePrHead
            )
        }

        # ============================================================
        # POSTCONDITIONS
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "postconditions"

        Invoke-CheckedNative `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "--prune"
            ) | Out-Null

        $VerifiedRemoteHead = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/$PrBranch"
            )

        if ($VerifiedRemoteHead -ne $LocalHead) {
            throw "Remote PR head did not match validated local HEAD after push."
        }

        $FinalPrJson = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "gh" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "pr",
                "view",
                "$PrNumber",
                "--repo",
                $Repository,
                "--json",
                "number,state,isDraft,headRefOid,url"
            )

        $FinalPr = $FinalPrJson | ConvertFrom-Json

        if ($FinalPr.headRefOid -ne $LocalHead) {
            throw "GitHub PR #100 does not point at the validated sync commit."
        }

        $FinalStatus = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($FinalStatus)) {
            throw "Working tree is not clean after PR update."
        }

        # ============================================================
        # DONE
        # ============================================================

        $CurrentPhase = "done"

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "[DONE] PR #100 UPDATED ONTO REPAIRED MAIN" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[STATE] Main integrated: $ExpectedMain"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Old PR head:     $OriginalPrHead"
        Write-Host "[STATE] New PR head:     $LocalHead"
        Write-Host "[STATE] PR:              $($FinalPr.url)"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Draft:           $($FinalPr.isDraft)"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Changed files:   6 exact intended files"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Local tests:     PASS"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Working tree:    CLEAN"
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[NEXT] GitHub Actions will rerun on the new exact PR head. Send me this output and I will inspect the checks."
    }
    catch {
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[ERROR] $($_.Exception.Message)" -ForegroundColor Red
        Show-RecoveryState
        throw
    }
}

This deliberately does not use rebase or force-push. It merges repaired main into the existing PR branch, preserving the PR history and giving us a normal new exact head for CI. It also recognizes a locally created-but-not-yet-pushed sync merge, so a failure after the merge does not force you to start over.

Yes. This failure exposed a real missing rule in the standard.

The command stopped during preflight, before branch switching, merging, editing, committing, or pushing, so nothing needs to be recovered.

I changed the approach so we don't depend on your interactive PowerShell execution policy at all. I made a one-shot updater script that will:

launch in its own child powershell.exe using -ExecutionPolicy Bypass;
merge repaired main (920bd7ca...) into PR #100;
update OPERATOR_COMMAND_STANDARDS.md with the execution-policy rule;
update OPERATOR_COMMAND_TEMPLATE.md so temporary Process-scope handling is explicit and restored in finally;
update the deterministic smoke test to enforce those rules;
prohibit persistent CurrentUser / LocalMachine policy weakening;
run the command-policy, native-command, checkout-root, TaskGraph, CI-split, compile, and whitespace tests;
verify PR #100 still has exactly its intended six files;
commit and push without force;
not merge PR #100.

Download the corrected PR #100 updater

Save/download it normally; then run this one command in PowerShell:

$Script = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath "$env:USERPROFILE\Downloads" -Filter "Update-PR100-ExecutionPolicy*.ps1" -File | Sort-Object LastWriteTimeUtc -Descending | Select-Object -First 1; if ($null -eq $Script) { throw "Update-PR100-ExecutionPolicy.ps1 was not found in Downloads." }; & powershell.exe -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File $Script.FullName

That invocation is itself the pattern the standard should teach: don't alter your normal PowerShell policy just to run our trusted repository tooling; launch the bounded script in a child PowerShell with an explicit process-only bypass.

**ChatGPT:**

This failure is the good kind. The updater refused to overwrite two uncommitted files from our earlier historical-context attempt. We should preserve them, not reset them.

Remote state is still stable: main is 920bd7ca..., and PR #100 is still open/draft at 7b68f887....

Run this block. It will:

Require exactly the branch/HEAD you showed.
Require exactly those two dirty files—nothing else.
Save a patch backup in Downloads.
Stash exactly those two files and verify the stash.
Update local main to the repaired 920bd7ca....
Verify the downloaded PR #100 updater by SHA-256.
Run it with execution-policy bypass.
Leave the preserved stash alone for us to inspect afterward.
& {
    $ErrorActionPreference = "Stop"

    $Root = "C:\NSC\NSC\NoSafeCircle"
    $ExpectedBranch = "fix/historical-context-scene-policy"
    $ExpectedHead = "f1ad90f6a4cee16b14f8dc313c8831fc7743d587"
    $ExpectedMain = "920bd7ca18e0c39cc81145a546956a8f804ef963"

    $ExpectedUpdaterHash =
        "9407fb24efe037265bba2846043ca85172fa247fb0eb887db5649a4fb30e978d"

    $PreservePaths = @(
        "Pipeline/TaskGraph/scene_path_policy.py",
        "Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent/tests/scene_path_contract_migration_smoke_test.py"
    ) | Sort-Object

    function Invoke-Native {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
            [string]$FilePath,

            [Parameter()]
            [string[]]$ArgumentList = @(),

            [Parameter()]
            [int[]]$AllowedExitCodes = @(0),

            [switch]$StreamOutput
        )

        $PreviousPreference = $ErrorActionPreference
        $Output = @()
        $ExitCode = -1

        try {
            $ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"

            if ($StreamOutput) {
                & $FilePath @ArgumentList 2>&1 |
                    ForEach-Object {
                        Write-Host $_
                    }

                $ExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
            }
            else {
                $Output = @(
                    & $FilePath @ArgumentList 2>&1
                )

                $ExitCode = $LASTEXITCODE
            }
        }
        finally {
            $ErrorActionPreference = $PreviousPreference
        }

        if ($AllowedExitCodes -notcontains $ExitCode) {
            if (-not $StreamOutput) {
                $Output |
                    ForEach-Object {
                        Write-Host $_
                    }
            }

            throw (
                $FilePath +
                " failed with exit code " +
                $ExitCode +
                "."
            )
        }

        return [pscustomobject]@{
            ExitCode = $ExitCode
            Output = $Output
        }
    }

    function Get-NativeText {
        param(
            [Parameter(Mandatory = $true)]
            [string]$FilePath,

            [Parameter()]
            [string[]]$ArgumentList = @()
        )

        $Result = Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath $FilePath `
            -ArgumentList $ArgumentList

        return (($Result.Output -join "`n").Trim())
    }

    $PatchPath = $null
    $PatchHash = $null
    $StashSha = $null

    try {
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host " PRESERVE OLD REPAIR + RESUME PR #100" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host ""

        # ============================================================
        # PREFLIGHT
        # ============================================================

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Root -PathType Container)) {
            throw "Repository root is missing: $Root"
        }

        Set-Location $Root

        $Branch = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "branch",
                "--show-current"
            )

        if ($Branch -ne $ExpectedBranch) {
            throw (
                "Unexpected branch. Expected " +
                $ExpectedBranch +
                ", found " +
                $Branch
            )
        }

        $Head = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "HEAD"
            )

        if ($Head -ne $ExpectedHead) {
            throw (
                "Unexpected HEAD. Expected " +
                $ExpectedHead +
                ", found " +
                $Head
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Expected abandoned local repair branch"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Branch: $Branch"
        Write-Host "[STATE] HEAD:   $Head"

        # ============================================================
        # FIND + VERIFY THE DOWNLOADED UPDATER BEFORE MUTATING ANYTHING
        # ============================================================

        $Updater = Get-ChildItem `
            -LiteralPath "$env:USERPROFILE\Downloads" `
            -Filter "Update-PR100-ExecutionPolicy*.ps1" `
            -File |
            Sort-Object LastWriteTimeUtc -Descending |
            Select-Object -First 1

        if ($null -eq $Updater) {
            throw "Update-PR100-ExecutionPolicy.ps1 was not found in Downloads."
        }

        $UpdaterHash = (
            Get-FileHash `
                -LiteralPath $Updater.FullName `
                -Algorithm SHA256
        ).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()

        if ($UpdaterHash -ne $ExpectedUpdaterHash) {
            throw (
                "Updater SHA-256 mismatch.`nExpected: " +
                $ExpectedUpdaterHash +
                "`nActual:   " +
                $UpdaterHash
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] PR #100 updater SHA-256 verified"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Updater: $($Updater.FullName)"

        # ============================================================
        # VERIFY REMOTE MAIN BEFORE PRESERVATION
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[READ] Refreshing origin" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "fetch",
                "origin",
                "--prune"
            ) | Out-Null

        $RemoteMain = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "origin/main"
            )

        if ($RemoteMain -ne $ExpectedMain) {
            throw (
                "origin/main moved again.`nExpected: " +
                $ExpectedMain +
                "`nActual:   " +
                $RemoteMain
            )
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Current repaired main verified: $RemoteMain"

        # ============================================================
        # PROVE EXACT DIRTY SCOPE
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[VERIFY] Exact local dirty-file scope" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $ModifiedResult = Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "diff",
                "--name-only",
                "HEAD",
                "--"
            )

        $ModifiedPaths = @(
            $ModifiedResult.Output |
                Where-Object {
                    -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
                } |
                Sort-Object -Unique
        )

        $UntrackedResult = Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "ls-files",
                "--others",
                "--exclude-standard"
            )

        $UntrackedPaths = @(
            $UntrackedResult.Output |
                Where-Object {
                    -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
                }
        )

        if ($UntrackedPaths.Count -ne 0) {
            Write-Host "[UNTRACKED]"
            $UntrackedPaths |
                ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host "  $_"
                }

            throw "Unexpected untracked files exist. Nothing was preserved or removed."
        }

        $Difference = @(
            Compare-Object `
                $PreservePaths `
                $ModifiedPaths
        )

        if ($Difference.Count -ne 0) {
            Write-Host "[EXPECTED]"
            $PreservePaths |
                ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host "  $_"
                }

            Write-Host "[ACTUAL]"
            $ModifiedPaths |
                ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host "  $_"
                }

            throw "Dirty-file set is not exactly the two expected historical repair files."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Exactly two known local repair files are dirty"

        # ============================================================
        # DURABLE PATCH BACKUP
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WORK] Writing durable patch backup" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $RecoveryRoot = Join-Path `
            "$env:USERPROFILE\Downloads\NoSafeCircleOutput" `
            "OperatorRecovery"

        if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $RecoveryRoot -PathType Container)) {
            New-Item `
                -ItemType Directory `
                -Path $RecoveryRoot `
                -Force |
                Out-Null
        }

        $Stamp = Get-Date -Format "yyyyMMdd-HHmmss"

        $PatchPath = Join-Path `
            $RecoveryRoot `
            "historical-context-scene-policy-local-$Stamp.patch"

        $PatchResult = Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "diff",
                "--binary",
                "--full-index",
                "HEAD",
                "--",
                $PreservePaths[0],
                $PreservePaths[1]
            )

        $PatchText = ($PatchResult.Output -join "`n")

        if ([string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($PatchText)) {
            throw "Expected local patch was empty."
        }

        [System.IO.File]::WriteAllText(
            $PatchPath,
            ($PatchText.TrimEnd("`n") + "`n"),
            (New-Object System.Text.UTF8Encoding($false))
        )

        $PatchHash = (
            Get-FileHash `
                -LiteralPath $PatchPath `
                -Algorithm SHA256
        ).Hash.ToLowerInvariant()

        Write-Host "[PASS] Patch backup created"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Patch:  $PatchPath"
        Write-Host "[STATE] SHA256: $PatchHash"

        # ============================================================
        # STASH EXACTLY THE TWO FILES
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WORK] Preserving exact local edits in Git stash" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        $StashMessage =
            "preserve historical-context-scene-policy local edits $Stamp"

        Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "stash",
                "push",
                "-m",
                $StashMessage,
                "--",
                $PreservePaths[0],
                $PreservePaths[1]
            ) | Out-Null

        $StashSha = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "stash@{0}"
            )

        $StashFilesResult = Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "stash",
                "show",
                "--name-only",
                "--format=",
                $StashSha
            )

        $StashFiles = @(
            $StashFilesResult.Output |
                Where-Object {
                    -not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($_)
                } |
                Sort-Object -Unique
        )

        if (@(Compare-Object $PreservePaths $StashFiles).Count -ne 0) {
            Write-Host "[STASH CONTENTS]"
            $StashFiles |
                ForEach-Object {
                    Write-Host "  $_"
                }

            throw "Preservation stash does not contain exactly the expected two files."
        }

        $AfterStash = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($AfterStash)) {
            throw "Working tree is still dirty after exact preservation stash."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Local edits preserved"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Stash SHA: $StashSha"
        Write-Host "[PASS] Working tree clean"

        # ============================================================
        # RETURN CONTROLLER TO CURRENT MAIN
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[WORK] Returning controller checkout to current main" -ForegroundColor Cyan

        Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "switch",
                "main"
            ) | Out-Null

        Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "merge",
                "--ff-only",
                "origin/main"
            ) | Out-Null

        $MainHead = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "rev-parse",
                "HEAD"
            )

        if ($MainHead -ne $ExpectedMain) {
            throw (
                "Local main did not reach expected repaired main: " +
                $MainHead
            )
        }

        $MainStatus = Get-NativeText `
            -FilePath "git" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "status",
                "--porcelain=v1",
                "--untracked-files=all"
            )

        if (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($MainStatus)) {
            throw "Local main is unexpectedly dirty."
        }

        Write-Host "[PASS] Controller main clean at $MainHead"

        # ============================================================
        # RESUME PR #100 UPDATER
        # ============================================================

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host " RESUMING PR #100 UPDATER" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Cyan
        Write-Host ""

        Invoke-Native `
            -FilePath "powershell.exe" `
            -ArgumentList @(
                "-NoProfile",
                "-ExecutionPolicy",
                "Bypass",
                "-File",
                $Updater.FullName
            ) `
            -StreamOutput | Out-Null

        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "[DONE] LOCAL REPAIR PRESERVED + PR #100 UPDATER COMPLETED" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Green
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "[STATE] Preserved stash: $StashSha"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Backup patch:    $PatchPath"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Patch SHA256:    $PatchHash"
        Write-Host "[STATE] Do NOT pop the stash yet."
        Write-Host "[NEXT] Send me this output so I can inspect PR #100 and then decide whether the preserved regression test should be retained."
    }
    catch {
        Write-Host ""
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "[BLOCKED] PRESERVATION / PR #100 RESUME STOPPED" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "============================================================" -ForegroundColor Yellow
        Write-Host "[ERROR] $($_.Exception.Message)"

        if ($null -ne $StashSha) {
            Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Preserved stash SHA: $StashSha"
        }

        if ($null -ne $PatchPath) {
            Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Backup patch: $PatchPath"
        }

        Write-Host "[RECOVERY] Do not reset, clean, or pop the stash."
        throw
    }
}

The important bit: we are preserving those local edits twice—once as a patch file and once as a Git stash. That gives us room to inspect whether the extra scene_path_contract_migration_smoke_test.py regression is worth keeping after PR #100 is settled.

And no, I would not update the standards because of this particular stop. “Refuse to proceed when the working tree contains unaccounted-for work” is exactly the rule we want. The standards change we still need is the execution-policy rule uncovered by the previous failure.

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Could not read current branch." }
    if ($Branch -ne $ExpectedBranch) {

    }

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Could not read HEAD." }
    if ($Head -ne $ExpectedHead) {

        throw "origin/main moved. Expected $ExpectedMain but found $RemoteMain."

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Could not switch to main." }

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Could not fast-forward main." }

    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Could not read final HEAD." }

    $FinalStatus = @(
        git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all

    Write-Host "[NEXT] Send me this output; then we can finish PR #102 and return to PR #100."
}

**ChatGPT:**

The publish itself succeeded. The repository was created, kept private, Issues were enabled, and exact Gauntlet commit 4318c840... was pushed as main; the only failure was the final “zero Issues” assertion. 

I found the bug: in Windows PowerShell 5.1, ConvertFrom-Json has a quirky behavior where JSON arrays can pass through the pipeline as a single array object. That means parsing GitHub’s perfectly valid [] and then wrapping it in @(...) can produce a count of 1 even though there are zero elements. Stack Overflow+1 (https://stackoverflow.com/questions/55575501/why-is-powershell-applying-the-predicate-of-a-where-to-an-empty-list?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

So do not delete or recreate the live repository and do not rerun the publish script. Run this small read-only verification instead:

$Repo="cathode26/TaskOrchestratorGauntletLive-20260831"; $CountText=(& gh issue list --repo $Repo --state all --limit 100 --json number --jq 'length'); if($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0){throw "Could not inspect live Gauntlet Issues."}; $Count=[int]$CountText.Trim(); Write-Host "[STATE] Actual GitHub Issue count: $Count"; if($Count -ne 0){& gh issue list --repo $Repo --state all --limit 100 --json number,title,state,url,author,createdAt; throw "The live Gauntlet really contains $Count Issue(s); send me the listing before continuing."}; Write-Host "[PASS] Live Gauntlet contains zero Issues. Publish checkpoint is valid."

I also checked through the GitHub connection from my side. The ChatGPT GitHub app currently has repository access to cathode26/NoSafeCircle only; the newly created private Gauntlet repo is not yet visible to me. That is why I cannot inspect its Issues directly through the connector yet.

After the command above reports 0, please also go to ChatGPT Apps → GitHub at https://chatgpt.com/apps and update the GitHub connection/repository access to include:

cathode26/TaskOrchestratorGauntletLive-20260831

**ChatGPT:**

Yes. This absolutely belongs in the command standards.

I’d record it as “PowerShell 5.1 JSON cardinality / pipeline-shape trap.” The concrete rule should be:

PROHIBITED for cardinality checks: @($Json | ConvertFrom-Json).Count
REQUIRED when the tool supports it: have the native tool return a scalar, e.g. gh ... --jq 'length', then validate/convert that scalar.
If we actually need the objects, parse JSON into a variable first and explicitly normalize null / array / single-object shapes before counting.
Add a deterministic Windows PowerShell 5.1 smoke fixture proving that [] cannot accidentally become “1 item.”

Your latest command also confirms the live Gauntlet has 0 Issues, so the publication itself is valid. We should not rerun or recreate anything.

**ChatGPT:**

This is the first real production defect the live Gauntlet has exposed.

NSC-604’s lease actually landed correctly: Issue #4 is agent_working, owned by gauntlet-bootstrap, with a valid lease ID and matching contract hash. Its single agent_lease_acquired event also matches that durable state and records the correct branch, source commit, checkout, and lease.

NSC-601–603 are also coherent: 601 reached repair after a three-event FAIL chain, 602 is the intended interrupted lease, and 603 is the intended pending human handoff.

The problem is production’s verification logic. After writing the event and Issue update, it does one immediate self.find(task_id) and fails if that single read does not already show the exact new state. GitHub apparently lagged briefly on NSC-604, so the worker reported failure even though its durable lease had succeeded.

I recorded that separately as NoSafeCircle Issue #104 — “TaskReviewAgent: tolerate bounded GitHub read-after-write lag.” We should fix that before the generic-worker wave.

For now, we can safely finish the bootstrap because it uses the same stable worker ID and is state-aware. I made a bounded recovery runner: it retries the bootstrap entry point only when the failure is specifically the known could not be verified false-negative, waits four seconds, and stops immediately on any other failure. It does not delete or rewrite the existing Issues.

**ChatGPT:**

Good catch by the parser preflight. Nothing executed, so the live repo, Issues, branches, and production state are unchanged.

The cause was exactly the PowerShell variable/punctuation rule:

"$Label: something"

is invalid; it must be:

"$($Label): something"

One line actually contained both $Label: and $TaskId:, which is why PowerShell reported nine parser errors.

I also logged this concrete regression in command-governance Issue #103 as item 6: variable interpolation directly before : requires an explicit boundary.

I corrected every occurrence and ran a static scan ensuring no unsafe $variable: patterns remain except valid scoped variables such as $env:....

Only the Unity-specific RepositoryScopeAuthority + ExecutionCrew implementation portion gets replaced by deterministic Fibonacci/dice/hybrid execution inside the test harness.

**ChatGPT:**

Exactly — but I’d call them synthetic execution workers, not fully fake workers.

We do not want to fake the parts we're trying to prove. The real orchestrator should still do:

generic worker starts → chooses task → resume/fresh decision → claims resources → acquires GitHub Issue lease → creates/resumes checkout → owns branch

Then, instead of handing the task to the Unity-specific ExecutionCrew, it sees that this is a Gauntlet synthetic task and hands execution to something like:

GauntletSyntheticExecutor

That executor can actually do the deterministic work:

Fibonacci task → calculate result and write Gauntlet/results/NSC-###.txt
Dice task → call the real deterministic dice harness, create the next immutable submission receipt
Hybrid task → read accepted upstream results and calculate its result
Shared-resource task → perform its synthetic operation while still relying on the real orchestrator resource claim
Then commit/push the result and use the real production human-handoff workflow

So conceptually:

                     REAL
Generic Worker
     ↓
Task selection
     ↓
Dependency checks
     ↓
Resource arbitration
     ↓
Atomic Git claims
     ↓
GitHub Issue lease
     ↓
Resume/create checkout
     ↓
       ┌───────────────────────┐
       │ Execution backend     │
       │                       │
       │ Unity task → Unity    │
       │ Gauntlet → Synthetic  │
       └───────────────────────┘
                    ↓
              commit + push
                    ↓
          real human-review state
                    ↓
            PASS / FAIL / resume

The distinction matters. If we made the whole worker fake, the Gauntlet would stop proving the important stuff: race handling, resume priority, Issue leases, resource conflicts, checkout recovery, branch ownership, and concurrency.

What we discovered is basically that our orchestration layer is becoming more generic than our execution layer. The traffic controller can dispatch arbitrary work, but right now every mechanic it sends out is a Unity mechanic. We need a lightweight synthetic mechanic specifically so we can stress-test the traffic controller.

And this is actually preferable for the ten-worker test. A Fibonacci/dice worker can finish in seconds instead of having ten Claude instances all chewing through fake Unity code. That lets us hammer the concurrency machinery much harder and much more cheaply.

So yes: we need fake/synthetic workers — but only from the implementation boundary downward. Everything above that boundary stays real.

**ChatGPT:**

Yes — we can work on that documentation in parallel, and I think we should. But there’s one important constraint: do not merge the documentation PR into production main until the Claude Stage 5 design job finishes.

Claude’s design launcher is pinned to production main 565898a8... and does a final check that origin/main has not moved. If we merge another PR while Claude is still working, its run will correctly stop before publishing its own branch. Creating a separate clone/branch, committing, pushing, and even opening a PR is fine; merging it is what we should delay.

And yes, I remember the additional prompt/command-generation problems we found after the earlier command-governance documentation. The newer set includes several nasty ones that absolutely belong in the durable docs/backlog:

PowerShell JSON cardinality: @($Json | ConvertFrom-Json).Count can report 1 for JSON []; use a native scalar such as gh --jq length.
Quoted native mini-language corruption: a PowerShell argument intended as something like join(",") reached the child as join(,).
Empty PowerShell array parameters: mandatory array parameters reject a valid empty array unless explicitly allowing empty collections.
$Variable: parser failures: generated strings such as $Label: and $TaskId: can fail parsing; use $($Label): / ${Label}:.
Compound native arguments splitting: constructing a refspec inline inside an argument array caused one semantic argument to become multiple child arguments. Precompute the complete argument into a variable first.
Verifier/schema drift: we had a generated verifier assume dispatch_plan.resume.workflow_state.phase even though the committed API is flattened as dispatch_plan.resume.phase.
Operators after command calls: Get-MachineText ... -split ... can be parsed as parameters to the command rather than an operator expression. Capture the command result first or parenthesize it.
.Trim() corrupting machine data: trimming git status --porcelain removed the significant leading space from " M path". Scalar-output helpers and exact-machine-output helpers need to be distinct.
We also strengthened prompt rules around bounded authority: no silent commits/pushes/Issue mutation/provider invocation/decomposition/autonomous dispatch unless that operation is explicitly part of the job.
And there was an important wording ambiguity around provider-internal repository search versus workflow-level approved_command_execution; those need to be described as different capabilities rather than lumped together.

Some of those are already recorded on Issue #103, but the durable operator documentation predates several of them. So yes, this is exactly a useful thing to do while the agents work.

I’d make this a third completely isolated track:

Codex Job 1
  → Gauntlet evidence/conformance

Claude
  → Stage 5/D1C design audit

Docs branch
  → consolidate all newly discovered
    prompt/command/operator failure classes

For the docs branch, I’d update the existing command/operator guidance rather than touch the new Stage 5 design folder Claude owns. Likely targets are Docs/AI-Pipeline/OPERATOR_FILE_HANDOFF_AND_DOWNLOADS.md plus a focused companion document for agent prompt/runner construction rules if the existing file is getting too broad.

We can commit, push, and review that PR while they run. We just leave it unmerged until Claude reports [DONE].

I think this is worth doing now because these failures are no longer random anecdotes — we have enough of them to turn them into a proper “known-safe patterns / prohibited patterns / failure examples” reference that the next ChatGPT, Claude, or Codex instance can follow instead of rediscovering them.

**ChatGPT:**

Done. I created a dedicated docs-only branch and opened draft PR #108:

PR #108 — Docs: add agent prompt and runner construction rules

The branch is docs/agent-prompt-runner-rules, based exactly on production 565898a80e16e2aaa7ecf78d024bca1197053aa4. The commit is 24e81073e41303a93e9f4d8a374ff805e10b4854. GitHub confirms the PR contains exactly one new documentation file, 622 lines, and zero code changes.

The new file is:

Docs/AI-Pipeline/AGENT_PROMPT_AND_RUNNER_CONSTRUCTION_RULES.md

It complements the existing operator handoff document, which already covers durable Downloads handoffs and the separation of deterministic setup from provider execution.

The new rulebook consolidates the failures we discovered and adds the broader agent-job lessons:

prompt authority boundaries: read/write/command/Git/GitHub/live-system authority are specified separately;
don't one-shot a giant lifecycle when two independently provable jobs are better;
prompt files + SHA verification instead of huge quote-heavy inline prompts;
mandatory PowerShell parser preflight;
$Variable: interpolation failure;
compound native argv splitting;
quote-bearing jq/native mini-language corruption;
ConvertFrom-Json empty-array cardinality trap;
mandatory empty-array parameter binding;
-split and other operators being parsed as command parameters;
.Trim() corrupting whitespace-sensitive machine formats;
verifier schema assumptions versus the producer's actual committed JSON shape;
stdout/stderr separation and native exit-code authority;
state-aware recovery after partial mutations;
host-side enforcement of model write boundaries;

Great. I reviewed Claude’s actual PR #107, not just the completion message.

The execution itself was clean: PR #107 is open, mergeable, based on the intended production commit, and contains exactly 8 new design documents with zero code changes. Its exact head is bee6643cc511baaa65643ef6379b39a808232597.

I would not merge #107 yet. Claude found the right overall architecture—Stage 5 is essentially D1C graph application + durable orchestrator integration, rather than rebuilding D1A/D1B—but I found four substantive design errors and left them as a durable review on PR #107.

The proposed global claim refs/nsc/claims/decomposition-apply-global cannot actually reuse the existing claim client “with zero new code.” Production GitRefClaimClient only constructs a task ref plus hashed resource refs. A better design is probably to claim a canonical resource such as:
logical:taskgraph-decomposition-apply-global

That uses the existing resource-claim machinery exactly as designed.

There is an internal contradiction around stale graph deltas. The current plan_id includes both the whole source graph hash and allocated child IDs. Claude's D1C design then requires the current graph hash to equal the stored one and the recomputed delta to be byte-identical to the reviewed delta. Therefore, after another decomposition changes the graph and consumes some IDs, an older approved proposal cannot simply recompute and shift its IDs. It is stale and must go back through proposal/review with a new plan_id.

That is important because Claude currently claims both behaviors.

Claude says D1C can rewrite an existing dependent task's depends_on while that task has an active implementation worker, because a later resume would notice the changed contract hash. I don't accept that. graph_delta.py actually increments the dependent's contract revision and rewrites its dependencies. The current stale-contract check happens when selecting an agent-ready resume candidate; it doesn't magically stop an already-running agent_working worker.

D1C therefore needs authority over every existing task contract it intends to rewrite, not merely the decomposition parent and resource strings.

Claude treated the GitHub read-after-write problem as essentially solved except for tuning. It isn't fully solved yet. goal_loop_guard.py::_release_active_lease() still does:
add_comment
→ update_issue
→ one immediate find()
→ exact compare

with no bounded reread loop. That's the remaining #104 class we already discovered. Stage 5 should explicitly depend on finishing that audit rather than copying the current one-shot path.

So the architecture is promising, but Claude needs a targeted revision, not another broad redesign.

Also, because Claude's pinned run is now finished, the reason we were holding PR #108 unmerged is gone. We can review/merge that agent-prompt/runner documentation independently now.

My next move would be to send Claude back into the same Stage 5 design branch with a very narrow assignment: fix only these four architecture contradictions, propagate the corrections consistently through all eight documents, and touch no code.

**ChatGPT:**

I reviewed the exact updated PR #107 head:

44baeb1ae1f6dcd4078b54e68336ca50509333af

GitHub confirms it is open, mergeable, two commits, and still exactly the eight documentation files.

The four original architecture problems are substantially fixed. In particular, the revised docs now correctly distinguish the existing logical-resource claim from the genuinely new multi-task claim work, and explicitly say the Gauntlet cannot prove that new primitive merely by passing.

But I would not merge #107 yet. The exact-head review found four smaller contradictions:

D1C would block its own parent lease as written. The affected set includes the parent, but the rule then says no affected task may be agent_working for any worker. During decomposition_apply, the parent should be agent_working for the current D1C worker. It needs to allow that exact expected parent lease while blocking unsafe ownership on rewritten dependents.
The filesystem atomicity claim is too strong. The doc says ordered os.replace means no partial state is visible until the last replacement, then immediately describes partial files existing after a crash. Per-file replacement is not an atomic multi-file transaction. The real guarantee should be: partial state may temporarily exist inside the isolated D1C checkout, but it is never committed/pushed/integrated, and recovery fails closed.
The idempotency fallback is impossible with current plan_graph_delta(). The design says an already-applied plan might be detected by “recomputing the plan and finding zero diff.” But successful application converts the parent to decomposed/not_applicable; current plan_graph_delta() explicitly requires decomposition_state == concrete and execution_scope == needs_execution_decomposition. The applied plan_id/child provenance should be the idempotency authority instead.
Two summaries weren't fully propagated. Slice 8 says it lists every race requiring a new mechanism, but lists #2, #10, #15 and omits corrected race #4, which is now the multi-task-claim race. Also, the executive README still says Slices 1–3 are GO before the Gauntlet and doesn't mention #104 in the top-level gate. That conflicts with our current project decision that Stage 5 implementation stays frozen until the Gauntlet passes; only design/audit runs in parallel.

There's also one small wording error: the global-claim section calls parent/affected dependents “other resources” in exclusive_resources; those are task refs, while the global logical token and actual resource strings are resource refs.

I put these findings directly on PR #107 as a GitHub review, so they're durable.

This is now small enough that I would not send Claude back again. I recommend one host-only documentation patch touching the necessary docs, validate it, push one final commit to #107, and then I can do the final merge review.

**You:**

If the gauntlet finds no bugs, then we wasted like 5 hours

* * *

**ChatGPT:**

Some of it, yes. I wouldn’t pretend otherwise.

If the Gauntlet runs perfectly and finds zero bugs, then part of these five hours was process overhead we could have avoided. We spent real time fighting launchers, Docker/Codex sandbox behavior, PowerShell JSON weirdness, model turn limits, and CI assumptions. Those things do not make your game better.

But the full five hours were definitely not wasted, because we already found and fixed real things before the Gauntlet even started:

The production #104 fix originally covered the obvious Issue workflow path but missed direct mutation/readback callers.
The downstream lifecycle had a merge-closeout checkout conflict fallthrough.
We did not have a full REQUEST_CHANGES → worker resumes → renewed durable review proof.
We did not have a real PR already merged but Issue completion crashed recovery proof.
The private Gauntlet CI was accidentally assuming production NSC-020/NSC-050 task fixtures existed.
We caught the dangerous assumption that an agent/provider failure means “it didn't modify anything.”
We found several weaknesses in our own orchestration around retries, partial work preservation, and validation authority.

Those are exactly the kinds of bugs the Gauntlet is supposed to expose. We effectively found some of them while constructing the launchpad.

The more important point is this: we are now at diminishing returns. I do not want another five-hour round of “make the Gauntlet safer before we run the Gauntlet.” After this independent review, if it is clean, we merge and run the damn thing.

And if the one-worker run and then the multi-worker Gauntlet pass with zero defects, I would interpret that as:

The pre-live adversarial work did its job. The orchestrator survived the test.

Not:

We should invent an even harder pre-Gauntlet review before trusting it.

There is also a useful lesson for the future. We have learned enough about the failure modes that the next version of this process should be dramatically cheaper. We should not need bespoke 500-line guarded launchers and three rounds of launcher repair every time. The eventual orchestrator should automate most of the exact pinning, independent review, CI gating, partial-work preservation, and live escalation that we have been doing manually.

So if it passes cleanly, I’ll be happy—but I’ll also say: good, stop polishing the testing machine and use the fucking machine to build the game.

# Verdict
CHANGES_REQUIRED

# Executive assessment

The production #104 resync is exact: all 13 requested paths have identical Git blob IDs and file modes to production commit 0596dea8258718208a968cb36c18a552d2366441. The PR also has exactly the expected 19-file scope, with no TaskGraph, task contract, Job 1 evidence API, fixture-generation, dispatch-policy, or Stage 5 changes.

Checkout-conflict handling and REQUEST_CHANGES resume are adequately closed. However, the already-MERGED crash/resume proof bypasses the production checkout observer. After the injected crash, the real checkout manager would report conflict, so the resumed controller would stop before inspecting the already-merged PR. The test passes only because MemoryWorkflow always reports the checkout as ready.

This is a HIGH merge-safety finding because one of the three required Job 2 hardenings is not actually reachable through the live production/Gauntlet composition, despite green CI.

# Blockers / high findings

- Severity: HIGH
- Exact code: [SyntheticDownstreamController._prepare_or_recover_checkout](/workspace/Gauntlet/synthetic_downstream.py:314), [SyntheticDownstreamController._merge_closeout](/workspace/Gauntlet/synthetic_downstream.py:935), [test_already_merged_pr_resumes_post_merge_completion_once](/workspace/Gauntlet/synthetic_downstream_smoke_test.py:786), and the fake checkout observation in [MemoryWorkflow](/workspace/Gauntlet/synthetic_downstream_smoke_test.py:72).
- Concrete failure scenario: the first green-check closeout commits and pushes the evidence commit, merges the PR, then crashes before Issue completion. The durable Issue still records the candidate commit because the evidence head is updated only through the pending-check release path. On a real fresh run, ResumableTaskCheckoutManager.inspect(...) sees local/remote branch HEAD at the evidence commit while workflow head_commit remains the candidate, returns conflict, and _prepare_or_recover_checkout fails before _inspect_or_merge can observe MERGED.
- Why tests/CI do not save it: MemoryWorkflow.checkout_observation remains hard-coded to ready, even after HEAD advances and origin/main changes. Thus the test bypasses the exact production checkout-status branch that prevents live resume. The same seam also overstates the existing materialization/push-failure recovery test.
- Smallest safe repair: durably checkpoint the exact evidence head in the managed Issue before invoking the merge boundary. This can be a verified release/resume boundary using the existing resumable downstream coordinator, or an equally durable hash-bound transition. A resumed workflow must then expect the evidence commit, allowing existing checkout machinery to return a legitimate recoverable status.
- Regression test required: repeat the already-MERGED crash using DownstreamTaskReviewWorkflow and the real ResumableTaskCheckoutManager, or a faithful production-manager composition. After the crash, prove the actual checkout inspection is recoverable, the resumed run observes MERGED, does not invoke gh pr merge again, evaluates fresh origin/main, and records exactly one production complete(...) event.

# Medium findings

None.

# Production #104 blob identity

13/13 exact. Every requested resync path has the same Git blob ID and file mode as production commit 0596dea8258718208a968cb36c18a552d2366441.

Outside the two intentional private fixture-test edits, the complete Pipeline/TaskReviewAgent tree also matches production.

# Three hardening findings

- Checkout conflict: Closed. _prepare_or_recover_checkout admits only ready, missing, and unmanaged_exact; conflict and unknown values fail before _merge_closeout. The regression creates an actual unresolved merge conflict, verifies UU status, and proves no evidence materialization, push, PR command, or closeout call occurs.
- REQUEST_CHANGES resume: Closed. The test uses production IssueWorkflowService and DownstreamIssueCoordinator transitions, reaches durable agent_ready / delivery_evidence, creates a fresh controller/worker, re-derives candidate authority, preserves the candidate commit count, safely reuses identical proposal bytes, publishes a new hash-bound BLOCKED event, and leaves the checkout clean.
- Already-MERGED crash/resume: Not closed. GitHub merge and fresh-main conformance behavior are otherwise credible, but the fresh controller receives a fabricated ready checkout status. The production checkout manager would report conflict because durable workflow head authority was never advanced from candidate to evidence commit.

# CI compatibility assessment

Only three production-fixture-dependent operations are guarded:

- The deterministic workflow skips only the real NSC-050 observation CLI when Tasks/NSC-050.yaml is absent.
- task_review_agent_smoke_test.py skips only test_real_repository_observation.
- downstream_determinism_smoke_test.py skips only the NSC-020 PlayMode-only policy assertion.

The remaining Core tests continue, Core relevance and trigger logic are unchanged, Delivery workflow is byte-identical to the base, and no production runtime module was changed for fixture compatibility. Both fixtures are genuinely absent privately and present in production.

The Python helpers blindly treat every nonzero git cat-file -e result as absence. An isolated Git object/read failure could therefore be mislabeled as SKIP for that one assertion. Both harnesses also print an outer PASS after the inner SKIP. These are narrow, non-production reporting weaknesses; they do not silently skip broader Core or Delivery behavior and are not independently merge-blocking.

# Authority/scope assessment

The PR changes exactly the expected 19 files. It does not change:

- Tasks/ or Pipeline/TaskGraph/;
- any Job 1 evidence API/document/test;
- fixture manifest, results, bootstrap, or generator;
- autonomous dispatch policy;
- production repository state;
- Stage 5.

LIVE_HARNESS.md truthfully records production commit 0596deaâ€¦, the private resync, production issue #104 remaining open pending private live acceptance, the blocked multi-worker wave, and the absence of a Gauntlet-specific visibility retry layer. It does not claim live acceptance occurred.

# Live-acceptance recommendation

Do not merge this exact PR head before the HIGH finding is repaired. The one-worker live proof needs a real durable evidence-head checkpoint so an already-merged crash can resume through production checkout authority.

**ChatGPT:**

Good review. We do not merge PR #8 at 77c60be....

Codex found one real HIGH: the already-merged recovery test was bypassing the real checkout observer, so a live restart could see candidate-vs-evidence as a checkout conflict and stop before discovering that the PR had already merged. 

The encouraging part is that the architecture already contains the fix. ResumableDownstreamIssueCoordinator.release_for_pending_checks(...) durably advances Issue head_commit to the evidence commit while deliberately preserving the original human_handoff_commit. The synthetic adapter currently uses that mechanism only when PR checks are pending; when checks are already green, it jumps directly into gh pr merge, which creates the hole Codex found.

So this repair is narrow: evidence must always be durably checkpointed before the first merge inspection. The first closeout will now stop successfully at an evidence_head_checkpointed boundary. A subsequent generic resume then goes through the real checkout manager with the evidence commit as durable head authority and can safely merge or discover an already-merged PR.
